, prepared independently by Alice and Bob, we will assume that there must exist a unique associated bipartite real state in
{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
. More explicitly, we will assume that there are maps

\iota^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}
which describe the inclusion of ’pure tensors’ in
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcrcl}\iota^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}&:&{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}&\longrightarrow&{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\\
&&(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})&\mapsto&\sigma_{A}\boxtimes\sigma_{B}.\end{array}

Thirdly, for every bipartite real states
\sigma_{AB},\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{AB}
such that
\sigma_{AB}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}
, we will assume that there must exist real effects
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B}
such that when Alice and Bob prepare
\sigma_{AB}
and apply real effects
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}
respectively, the resulting determination (fixed as the product of the separate determinations) is different from the experiment where Alice and Bob prepare
\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}
and apply
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}
respectively. As a summary, applying real effects locally is sufficient to distinguish all of the states in
{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}
(this principle is called ”tomographic locality”), i.e.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\boxtimes\sigma_{i,B}\right)\in\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}},\;\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\boxtimes\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\right)\in\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}},

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left(\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},\;\;\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{i,B})=\bigwedge{}_{j\in J}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\;\Rightarrow\;(\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\boxtimes\sigma_{i,B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\boxtimes{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\boxtimes\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.7)

Fourthly, we impose that if Alice (or Bob) prepares a mixture of states, then this results in a mixture of the respective bipartite states. More explicitly, we require, for any
\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
,
\{\,\sigma_{j,B}\;|\;j\in J\,\}\subseteq{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
,
\sigma_{A}\in{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{B}\in{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
, the two bimorphic properties for the tensor product :

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,A})\boxtimes\sigma_{B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}\boxtimes\sigma_{B}),

(4.8)

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}\boxtimes(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,B})=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{A}\boxtimes\sigma_{i,B}).

(4.9)

Endly, we will assume the existence of the two partial traces

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\textit{\rm there exists an homomorphism denoted $\zeta^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{(1)}$ from $\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}$ to $\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}$}\\
\textit{\rm there exists an homomorphism denoted $\zeta^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{(2)}$ from $\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}$ to $\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}$}\end{array}\right.

4.2.
The minimal tensor product

We now recall some basic elements about the construction of the minimal tensor product of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}

[
12
]
. The two states/effects Chu Spaces
({\mathfrak{S}}_{A},{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}},\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}})
and
({\mathfrak{S}}_{B},{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}},\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}})
are equipped respectively with their real structure denoted by
(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},\star)
and
(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)
. Here again, we will denote
{\mathfrak{E}}_{A}:=\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}
and
{\mathfrak{E}}_{B}:=\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}
.

Definition 4.1
.

The set
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
is equipped with the Inf semi-lattice structure
\cup
and with the following Inf semi-lattice morphisms defined for any
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B}
,

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\begin{array}[]{rcrcl}&&\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}:{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})&\longrightarrow&{\mathfrak{B}}\\
&&\{\,(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\,\}&\mapsto&\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}\;{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{i,B}).\end{array}

Definition 4.2
.

{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
is equipped with a congruence relation defined between any two elements
u_{AB}
and
u^{\prime}_{AB}
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
by

\displaystyle(\,u_{AB}\approx u^{\prime}_{AB}\,)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\,\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},\;\;\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(u_{AB})=\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(u^{\prime}_{AB})\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.16)

Definition 4.3
.

The space
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
is built as the quotient of
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
under the congruence relation
\approx
.

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{AB}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma_{AB}}:=\{\,u_{AB}\;|\;\sigma_{AB}\approx u_{AB}\,\}.

(4.17)

The map
\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}
will be abusively defined as a map from
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}
by
\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\widetilde{\sigma_{AB}}):=\nu\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}({\sigma_{AB}})
for any
\sigma_{AB}
in
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
.

The element
\widetilde{u}\in{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
associated to the element
u:=\{\,(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\,\}\in{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
will be denoted
\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}
.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists a map denoted
\iota^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}
from
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{\times}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
. We have explicitly

\displaystyle\iota^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\widetilde{(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})}.

(4.18)

We will adopt the basic notation
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}:=\iota^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})

Lemma 4.5
.

By construction, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-51.21504pt\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)\in{{\overline{S}}}_{AB},\;\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\right)\in{{\overline{S}}}_{AB},

\displaystyle\hskip-51.21504pt\left(\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},\;\;\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{i,B})=\bigwedge{}_{j\in J}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 142.26378pt\;\Rightarrow\;(\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.19)

Theorem 4.6
.

For any
\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
,
\{\,\sigma_{j,B}\;|\;j\in J\,\}\subseteq\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
,
\sigma_{A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
, we have the two following fundamental properties

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,A})\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}),

(4.20)

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,B})=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}).

(4.21)

Proof.

Using the homomorphic property for
{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and the distributivity property between
\bullet
and
\wedge
in
{\mathfrak{B}}
, we deduce

\displaystyle{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{B})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}\;{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A}))\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{B})

(4.22)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}\;(\,{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{B})),

and then, using Lemma
4.5
, we obtain the property (
4.20
). We obtain the property (
4.21
) along the same lines of proof.
∎

Definition 4.7
.

{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
is equipped with a partial order defined according to

\displaystyle\forall\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB},\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}\in{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}},\;\;\;\;(\,\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}\,)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

(4.23)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt(\,\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},\;\;{\nu}\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB})\leq{\nu}\,{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB})\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

Lemma 4.8
.

Let us consider
u_{AB}:=\{\,(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\,\}
an element of
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
. We have explicitly, for any
\sigma_{A}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
, the following equivalence

\displaystyle\left(\widetilde{u_{AB}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})}\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left((\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in I}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{B}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\right.

(4.24)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\left.\left(\forall\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I,\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{B}\right)\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

It is recalled that
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
are down-complete Inf semi-lattice and then the infima in this formula are well-defined.

Proof.

We intent to expand the inequality
\widetilde{u_{AB}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})}
. It is equivalent to

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},

\displaystyle\left(\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}\;{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{i,B})\right)\leq{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{B}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.25)

We intent to choose a pertinent set of effects
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A}

and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B}
to reformulate this inequality.

Let us firstly choose
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}={\mathfrak{Y}}_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{B}}}
. Using (
2.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\;\sigma_{i,A})\leq{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{A}),\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},

(4.26)

which leads immediately

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\;\sigma_{i,A}\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{A}.

(4.27)

Choosing
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}={\mathfrak{Y}}_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{A}}}
, we obtain along the same line

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\;\sigma_{i,B}\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{B}.

(4.28)

Let us now consider
\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I
and let us choose the real effects
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}
as follows

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}:={\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot\,,\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})}

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}:={\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot\,,\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})}

(4.29)

Note that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}
are
real
effects because
\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

We deduce, from the assumption (
4.25
), that, for this
\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I
, we have

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A}\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{A})\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B}\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{B}).

(4.30)

We let the reader check that we have obtained the whole set of independent inequalities reformulating the property (
4.25
).
∎

Theorem 4.9
.

{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
is a down-complete Inf semi-lattice with

\displaystyle\forall\{\,u_{i}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}),

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\;\widetilde{u_{i}}=\widetilde{\bigcup{}_{i\in I}\;u_{i}}.

(4.31)

Theorem 4.10
.

If
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
admit
\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and
\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
respectively as bottom elements, then

{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
admits a bottom element explicitly given by
\bot_{{}_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}=\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
.

Proof.

Trivial using the expansion (
4.24
).
∎

Theorem 4.11
.

We have the two following homomorphisms

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\begin{array}[]{rcrclccrcrcl}&&\zeta^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{(1)}:\;\;\;\;\;\;{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}&\longrightarrow&\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}&&&&&\zeta^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{(2)}:\;\;\;\;\;\;{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}&\longrightarrow&\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\\
&&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}&\mapsto&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}&&&&&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}&\mapsto&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,B}\end{array}

4.3.
Minimal tensor product vs. canonical tensor product

In the present subsection we recall some basic elements of the canonical construction for tensor product and we analyze its relation with our own construction of tensor product presented in the last subsection.

Definition 4.12
.

[
18
]

The canonical tensor product denoted
{S}_{AB}:=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\otimes\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
of the two Inf semi-lattices
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
is obtained as the solution of the following universal problem : there exists a bi-homomorphism, denoted
\iota
from
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to
{S}_{AB}
, such that, for any Inf semi-lattice
{\mathfrak{S}}
and any bi-homomorphism
f
from
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to
{\mathfrak{S}}
, there is a unique homomorphism
g
from
{S}_{AB}
to
{\mathfrak{S}}
with
f=g\circ\iota
. We denote
\iota(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})=\sigma\otimes\sigma^{\prime}
for any
\sigma\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

The tensor product
{S}_{AB}
exists and is unique up to isomorphism, it is built as the homomorphic image of the free
\sqcap
semi-lattice generated by the set
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
under the congruence relation determined by identifying
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime})
with
(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime})\sqcap(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime})
for all
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
and identifying
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{2})
with
(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}_{1})\sqcap(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}_{2})
for all
\sigma\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\sigma^{\prime}_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{2}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

Then, from now on,
{S}_{AB}
is the Inf semi-lattice (the infimum of
S\subseteq{S}_{AB}
will be denoted
\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{S}_{AB}}}S
) generated by the elements
\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B}
with
\sigma_{A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\sigma_{B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
and subject exclusively to the conditions

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\sigma_{A}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A})\otimes\sigma_{B}=(\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B})\sqcap_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B}),\\
\sigma_{A}\otimes(\sigma_{B}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{B})=(\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B})\sqcap_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}(\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma^{\prime}_{B}).\end{array}\right.

The space
{S}_{AB}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\otimes\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
is turned into a partially ordered set with the following binary relation

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{AB},\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}\in{S}_{AB},\;\;\;\;(\,\sigma_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}\,)

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\,\sigma_{AB}\sqcap_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{AB}=\sigma_{AB}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.38)

Definition 4.13
.

A non-empty subset
{\mathfrak{R}}
of
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
is called a bi-filter iff

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{A},\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{2,A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\forall\sigma_{B},\sigma_{1,B},\sigma_{2,B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},

\displaystyle(\,(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B})\leq(\sigma_{2,A},\sigma_{2,B})\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B})\in{\mathfrak{R}}\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\sigma_{2,A},\sigma_{2,B})\in{\mathfrak{R}},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.39)

\displaystyle(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{B}),(\sigma_{2,A},\sigma_{B})\in{\mathfrak{R}}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\sigma_{1,A}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{2,A},\sigma_{B})\in{\mathfrak{R}},

(4.40)

\displaystyle(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{1,B}),(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{2,B})\in{\mathfrak{R}}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{1,B}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{2,B})\in{\mathfrak{R}}.

(4.41)

Definition 4.14
.

If
\{(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B}),\cdots,(\sigma_{n,A},\sigma_{n,B})\}
is a non-empty finite subset of
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
, then the intersection of all bi-filters of
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
which contain
(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B})
,
\cdots
,
(\sigma_{n,A},\sigma_{n,B})
is a bi-filter, which we denote by
{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B}),\cdots,(\sigma_{n,A},\sigma_{n,B})\}
.

Lemma 4.15
.

If
F
is a filter of
{S}_{AB}
then the set
\alpha(F):=\{\,(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B}\in F\;\}
is a bi-filter of
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

Lemma 4.16
.

[
19
, Lemma 1]

Let us choose
\sigma_{A},\sigma_{1,A},\cdots,\sigma_{n,A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{B},\sigma_{1,B},\cdots,\sigma_{n,B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})\in{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{1,A},\sigma_{1,B}),\cdots,(\sigma_{n,A},\sigma_{n,B})\}

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{S}_{AB}}}_{1\leq i\leq n}\;\sigma_{i,A}\otimes\sigma_{i,B}\right)\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.42)

Lemma 4.17
.

[
19
, Theorem 1]

Let us choose
\sigma_{A},\sigma_{1,A},\cdots,\sigma_{n,A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{B},\sigma_{1,B},\cdots,\sigma_{n,B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
. Then,

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{S}_{AB}}}_{1\leq i\leq n}\;\sigma_{i,A}\otimes\sigma_{i,B}\right)\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}\sigma_{A}\otimes\sigma_{B}\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\,\textit{\rm there exists a n$-$ary lattice polynomial $p$}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}p(\sigma_{1,A},\cdots,\sigma_{n,A})

\displaystyle\textit{\rm and}\;\;\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}p^{\ast}(\sigma_{1,B},\cdots,\sigma_{n,B})\,).

(4.43)

where
p^{\ast}
denotes the lattice polynomial obtained from
p
by dualizing the lattice operations.

Definition 4.18
.

We denote by
{\overline{S}}{}^{fin}_{AB}
the sub-poset of
{\overline{S}}_{AB}
formed by finite infima of pure tensors.
It is also a sub- Inf semi-lattice of
{\overline{S}}_{AB}
.

Definition 4.19
.

We denote by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\,(\sigma^{\prime}_{A},\sigma^{\prime}_{B})\;|\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{B}\}

Theorem 4.20
.

We have the following obvious property relating the partial orders of
{{\overline{S}}}{}^{fin}_{AB}
and
{S}_{AB}
. For any
\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}\subseteq_{fin}S_{AB}
,

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{S}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\otimes\sigma_{i,B})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{S}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A}\otimes\sigma^{\prime}_{B}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\overline{S}}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{B}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.45)

Proof.

First of all, it is clear that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
is a bi-filter.

Secondly, it is easy to check that
(\sigma_{k,A},\sigma_{k,B})\in\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
for any
k\in I
. Indeed, for any
K\subseteq I
, if
k\in K
we have
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{l\in K}\;\sigma_{l,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{k,A}
and if
k\notin K
we have
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{k,B}
.

As a conclusion, and by definition of
{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
as the intersection of all bi-filters containing
(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})
for any
i\in I
, we have then

\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}\supseteq{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
.

We now use Lemma
4.16
to obtain the announced result.
∎

Theorem 4.21
.

If
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}

or

\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
are distributive (for example as simplex spaces of states), then
{{\overline{S}}}{}^{fin}_{AB}
and
{S}_{AB}
are isomorphic posets.

Proof.

We suppose that
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
or
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
is distributive and we intent to prove that
{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}=\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
for any
\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}\subseteq_{fin}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\times\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

Let us prove the following fact : every bi-filter
F
which contains
(\sigma_{k,A},\sigma_{k,B})
for any
k\in I
contains also
\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
. In fact, we can show that, for any bi-filter
F
we have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(\forall k\in I,\;(\sigma_{k,A},\sigma_{k,B})\in F)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A},\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{K^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{m\in I-K^{\prime}}\;\sigma_{m,B})\in F,

\displaystyle\forall{\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\subseteq 2^{I},{\mathcal{K}}\cup{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=2^{I},{\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=\varnothing,\{\varnothing\}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime},I\in{\mathcal{K}}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.46)

The first step towards (
4.46
) is obtained by checking that
\forall{\mathcal{K}},{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}\subseteq 2^{I},{\mathcal{K}}\cup{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=2^{I},{\mathcal{K}}\cap{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}=\varnothing,\{\varnothing\}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime},I\in{\mathcal{K}}
,

\displaystyle(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{m\in I-K^{\prime}}\;\sigma_{m})\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k})

(4.47)

for any distributive
{\mathfrak{S}}
and any collection of elements of
{\mathfrak{S}}
denoted
\sigma_{k}
for
k\in I
for which these two sides of inequality exist. To check this fact, we have to note that, using
[
6
, Lemma 8 p. 50]
, we have first of all

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k})=\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\!\!\!\left\{\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\pi_{K}(A)\;|\;A\in\prod_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}K\right\},

(4.48)

where
\pi_{K}
denotes the projection of the component indexed by K in the cardinal product
\prod_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}K
. Moreover, for any
A\in\prod_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}K
, there exists
L\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}
such that
\bigcup\{\pi_{K}(A)\;|\;K\in{\mathcal{K}}\}\;\supseteq\;(I\smallsetminus L)
and then
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\pi_{K}(A))\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{m\in I-L}\;\sigma_{m})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{K^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{K}}^{\prime}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{m\in I-K^{\prime}}\;\sigma_{m})
. As a result, we obtain the property (
4.47
).

The second step towards (
4.46
) consists in showing that

\displaystyle(\forall k\in I,\;(\sigma_{k,A},\sigma_{k,B})\in F)

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A},\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,B})\;\in F\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.49)

for any
{\mathcal{K}}\subseteq 2^{I}
. This intermediary result is obtained by induction on the complexity of the polynomial
(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{K\in{\mathcal{K}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})
by using the following elementary result

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{A},\sigma^{\prime}_{A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\sigma_{B},\sigma^{\prime}_{B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\;\;\;\;\left((\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B}),(\sigma^{\prime}_{A},\sigma^{\prime}_{B})\in F\right)\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\sigma_{A}\sqcup_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A},\sigma_{B}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{B})\in F\\
(\sigma_{A}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A},\sigma_{B}\sqcup_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{B})\in F\end{array}\right.

trivially deduced using the bi-filter character of
F
, i.e. properties (
4.39
)(
4.40
)(
4.41
).

As a final conclusion, using the explicit definition of
{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
as the intersection of all bi-ideals containing
(\sigma_{k,A},\sigma_{k,B})
for any
k\in I
, we obtain
\widetilde{\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}={\mathfrak{F}}\{(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}
.

{{\overline{S}}}{}^{fin}_{AB}
and
{S}_{AB}
are then isomorphic posets.
∎

Theorem 4.22
.

If
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}

and

{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
are simplex spaces of states, then
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
is also a simplex space of states. In other words, the bipartite experiments based on a pair
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
of deterministic spaces of states are described by a deterministic space of states obtained as the canonical tensor product
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
(Indeed, using Theorem
4.21
, we have also
{{\overline{S}}}{}^{fin}_{AB}={S}_{AB}
in this situation).

In that case, the explicit expression for the supremum of two elements in
{{\overline{S}}}{}^{fin}_{AB}
is given by

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})\sqcup{}_{{}_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B})=

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I,\;j\in J}\;(\sigma_{i,A}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A})\widetilde{\otimes}(\sigma_{i,B}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(4.52)

Proof.

If
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
were not a simplex, it would mean that we would have a family
(\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})_{i\in I}
of pure states of
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
, and another pure state
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
such that

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq_{{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}}

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}

(4.53)

but with

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}

\displaystyle\notin

\displaystyle\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\;|\;i\in I\,\}.

(4.54)

However, using the expansion (
4.24
) with (
4.53
), we deduce
\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,B}
and
\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}
and
\forall\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I,\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}
. Now, we use the simplexity of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
to deduce that
\sigma_{B}\in\{\,\sigma_{i,B}\;|\;i\in I\,\}
and
\sigma_{A}\in\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in I\,\}
and
\forall\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I,\;\;\sigma_{A}\in\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in K\,\}\;\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\;\sigma_{B}\in\{\,\sigma_{i,B}\;|\;i\in I-K\,\}
. We then obtain immediately the following result : there exists a unique
i\in I
such that
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}
. We have then obtained a contradiction. As a conclusion,
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
is a simplex.

Endly, the formula (
4.52
) is a direct consequence of the distributivity in
{{\overline{S}}}_{AB}
(see
[
19
, Theorem 3]
).
∎

4.4.
Bipartite deterministic experiments

During this subsection, we intent to show that the minimal tensor product is adequate for the description of bipartite deterministic experiments.
Here,
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
will be two deterministic spaces of states (i.e. they are simplex spaces of states). They are naturally equipped with their real structures described in (
2.120
).

Theorem 4.23
.

The minimal tensor product is adequate for the description of bipartite deterministic experiments.

Proof.

By assumption,
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
are deterministic spaces of states and then
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
admit trivial real structures (see property (
2.120
) ) with
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
. We then consider
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

First of all, we observe that the requirements (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) are direct consequences of Theorem
4.9
and Theorem
4.10
.

Then, because of the Theorem
4.22
, we know that
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\boxtimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
is also a deterministic space of states, and then
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}{\boxtimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
admits a real structure according to (
2.120
). This real structure
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
is simply given by
\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\boxtimes{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
and the star
\star
is the involution defined in (
2.120
).

Secondly, the requirement (
4.1
) is guarantied by Lemma
4.4
and the basic properties
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

Thirdly, the requirements (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) are guarantied by the properties (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) using
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}=\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
.

Endly, the requirement (
4.1
) is guarantied by Theorem
4.11
.
∎

4.5.
Morphisms for the bipartite deterministic experiments

Let us consider
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}
,
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}
,
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}
four simplex spaces of states. Let us consider a morphism
f
(resp.
g
) from the states space
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}
) to the states space
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}
).

We can define the morphism
(f\widetilde{\otimes}g)
from the simplex states space
{{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}={\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}
to the simplex states space
{{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}={\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}
by

\displaystyle(f\widetilde{\otimes}g)(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B_{1}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}}}_{i\in I}f(\sigma_{i,A_{1}})\widetilde{\otimes}g(\sigma_{i,B_{1}}).

(4.55)

5.
Ontic completions and contextuality

In this section, we will clarify the concept of compatibility between real measurement operators and construct the concept of operational description, which will lead us to establish the concept of contextuality. Throughout our demonstration, we will observe how generalized spaces of states with hidden states are not only obtained naturally by a completion procedure from real indeterministic spaces of states (see Section
3
), but are also linked to
contextual
empirical models associated with the operational descriptions of these real spaces of states. The notions relative to contextuality presented here are directly inspired by the notions developed in
[
1
]
.

5.1.
Notions relative to contextuality

During this subsection, the states/effects Chu Space
({\mathfrak{S}},{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}}},\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}})
will be equipped with a real structure denoted by
(\overline{\mathfrak{S}},\star)
. As before,
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}
is defined as the sub Inf semi-lattice of
{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
formed by the elements of

\displaystyle\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}\;|\;\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\},\sigma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma^{\star}\,\}\cup\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}}\,\}

We recall that,
{\mathfrak{B}}
being a simplex, the
N-
th tensor product
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}
is also a simplex (cf Lemma
4.22
). The space
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}
is then naturally equipped with a real structure (cf formula (
2.120
)). Hence, the reduced space of effects
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}}}
is naturally defined.

Definition 5.1
.

N
real measurement maps
{\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N}\in\overline{\mathfrak{M}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
are said to be
jointly compatible
iff there exists a map
\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}
, called
joint morphism
, defined from
{\mathfrak{S}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}
and satisfying

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in{\mathfrak{S}},

\displaystyle\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})=\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}(\sigma_{1})\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}}}\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}(\sigma_{2}),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.1)

\displaystyle\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}^{\ast}(\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}}})\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}},

(5.2)

\displaystyle\forall i=1,\cdots,N,

\displaystyle{\phi}_{i}=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\cdots{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(i)}\circ\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}.

(5.3)

We recall that
\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\cdots{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(i)}
is the partial trace projecting the tensor product
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}N}
on its
i-
th component. Here, we have denoted by
\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}^{\ast}
the right component of the Chu morphism
(\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}},\Psi_{{}_{({\phi}_{1},\cdots,{\phi}_{N})}}^{\ast})
.

Definition 5.2
.

A
compatibility context

U
is defined to be a subset of
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
such that the elements of
\{\,{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}\in U\,\}
form a family of jointly compatible real measurements. A
compatibility cover

{\mathcal{C}}
is defined as a family of
maximal compatibility contexts
covering the whole set of real measurements. In other words,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\bigcup_{{}_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}}C)=\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\\
\forall C,C’\in{\mathcal{C}},\;\;\;C\subseteq C’\;\Rightarrow\;C=C’\\
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\;\textit{\rm the elements of}\;\{\,{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\,\}\;\textit{\rm are jointly compatible}.\end{array}\right.

Let us briefly clarify the situation for
{\mathfrak{S}}
being a deterministic space of states.

Theorem 5.3
.

Let
{\mathfrak{S}}
be a simplex.

We note that
{\mathfrak{S}}=\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
,
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}={\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
and all measurements are real.

Let us consider any two measurement morphisms
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}
. These morphisms are necessarily compatible.

Proof.

We define the map
\Psi
as a map from
{\mathfrak{S}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}
which satisfies, for any
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}
,

\displaystyle\Psi(\sigma)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{\sigma^{\prime}\in\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}}\Psi(\sigma^{\prime})

(5.8)

and for any
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}=Max({\mathfrak{S}})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\bot,\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\bot\;\textit{\rm and}\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\bot,\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\bot\;\textit{\rm and}\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf N}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\textit{\bf Y},\\
\Psi(\sigma)=\bot\widetilde{\otimes}\bot&\textit{\rm if}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)=\bot\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma)=\bot.\end{array}\right.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

We observe that
\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}})}
is unambiguously defined by (
5.8
) because
{\mathfrak{S}}
is a simplex and then satisfies (
2.115
).

Secondly,
\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}})}
is a morphism by construction.

Endly, we can check easily for any
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}
(the proof begins on each expressions of (
5.1
) by a simple computation and is extended to the whole set of elements of
{\mathfrak{S}}
using the expression (
5.8
) and the homomorphic properties of
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}
)

\displaystyle(\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(1)}\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}})})(\sigma)={\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}}(\sigma)

(5.19)

\displaystyle(\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(2)}\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}})})(\sigma)={\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}}(\sigma).

(5.20)

\Psi
is then the joint morphism
\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma^{\prime}_{1})}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma_{2},\sigma^{\prime}_{2})}})}
.
∎

Theorem 5.4
.

If
{\mathfrak{S}}
is a simplex then the whole set of elements of
\{\,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}\;|\;{{\mathfrak{l}}}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\,\}
are jointly compatible measurement maps. In other words, the compatibility cover
{\mathcal{C}}
is then reduced to the singleton
\{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\}
.

Proof.

Straightforward using the generalization of the construction used in previous Theorem for the joint-morphism.
∎

Let us now come back to the generic case for
{\mathfrak{S}}
. We intent to clarify the structure of contexts.

Lemma 5.5
.

If the
N-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible, then the
(N+1)-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly-compatible, for any
{\mathfrak{l}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}\in{\mathfrak{E}}
.

Proof.

Let us consider that the
N-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible. There exists a joint morphism
\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}
satisfying the required conditions mentioned in Definition
5.1
. Using the map
\varphi
defined as a morphism from
{\mathfrak{B}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}2}
by

\displaystyle\varphi(\textit{\bf Y}):=\textit{\bf Y}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf Y}

\displaystyle\varphi(\textit{\bf N}):=\textit{\bf N}\widetilde{\otimes}\textit{\bf N}

(5.21)

we then build a map denoted
\Phi
from
{\mathfrak{S}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}(N+1)}
by

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(\varphi\widetilde{\otimes}id^{\widetilde{\otimes}(N-1)})\circ\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}

(5.22)

It is easy to check that
\Phi
is a joint morphism for the
(N+1)-
uple of measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
. In other words,

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}.

(5.23)

∎

Lemma 5.6
.

If the
N-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible, then the
(N+1)-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are also jointly-compatible.

Proof.

We first note that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{\,\mathfrak{l}\,}}=\omega\circ{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}

(5.24)

with

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in{\mathfrak{B}},\;\omega(\alpha):=\overline{\,\alpha\,}.

(5.25)

On another part, using the Lemma
5.5
, we know that there exists a joint morphism for for the
(N+1)-
uple of measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
, it is denoted
\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}
.

We then define the map denoted
\Phi^{\prime}
from
{\mathfrak{S}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}(N+1)}
by

\displaystyle\Phi

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle(\omega\widetilde{\otimes}id^{\widetilde{\otimes}N})\circ\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}.

(5.26)

We check easily that
\Phi
is a joint morphism for the
(N+1)-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
. In other words,

\displaystyle\Phi^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})}}.

(5.27)

∎

Lemma 5.7
.

For any
\eta\in{\mathfrak{B}}
, we define the morphism
\tau_{\eta}
as the constant map from
{\mathfrak{S}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}
sending any state to
\eta
.

If the
N-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible, then the
(N+1)-
uple of real measurement maps
(\tau_{\eta},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible.

Proof.

Direct consequence of Lemma
5.5
using the property

\displaystyle\tau_{\eta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau_{\eta}\circ{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}.

(5.28)

∎

Lemma 5.8
.

For any
\eta\in{\mathfrak{B}}
, if the
N-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible (
N\geq 2
), then the
(N+1)-
uple of real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},\cdots,{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{N}})
are jointly compatible.

Proof.

Let us fix
N=2
for simplicity.

First of all, we remark that, if we denote by
\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}
the joint morphism associated to the compatible real measurement maps
({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})
, the property (
5.2
), i.e.
\Psi_{{}_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}}^{\ast}(\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{{\mathfrak{B}}^{\widetilde{\otimes}2}}})\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
implies immediately

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}.

(5.29)

Let us then consider the morphism denoted
\Omega
and defined from
{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}
to
{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}
as follows. For any
\sigma\in{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}
, we adopt the writing of
\sigma
in terms of pure states

\sigma=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{i\in I}\alpha_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta_{i})
with
\alpha_{i},\beta_{i}\in\{\,\textit{\bf Y}\,,\,\textit{\bf N}\,\}
, then
\Omega(\sigma)
is defined by

\displaystyle\Omega(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{i\in I}\alpha_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta_{i}):=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{i\in I}(\alpha_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta_{i}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{B}}}}\beta_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{i}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta_{i})

(5.30)

The map
\Omega
is build as a morphism Moreover, we have immediately

\displaystyle\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(2)}\circ\Omega\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(1)}\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}={\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},

(5.31)

\displaystyle\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(3)}\circ\Omega\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{2}\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}={\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}},

(5.32)

\displaystyle\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}{\mathfrak{B}}}}_{(1)}\circ\Omega\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}={\mathfrak{m}}_{({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})}.

(5.33)

Then,
\Omega\circ\Psi_{({\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})}
is a joint morphism for the triple
({\mathfrak{m}}_{({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})},{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}})
.

As a result,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{1}}
,
{\mathfrak{m}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}}
and
{\mathfrak{m}}_{({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})}
are jointly compatible.
∎

We can then summarize Lemma
5.8
, Lemma
5.6
and Lemma
5.7
in terms of the following theorem on contexts :

Theorem 5.9
.

Let
C\in{\mathcal{C}}
designate a maximal compatibility context, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\in C\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\bot_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\in C\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\in C,

(5.34)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;\;\overline{\;\mathfrak{l}\;}\in C,

(5.35)

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{1},{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}\in C,\;\;({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})\in C.

(5.36)

We note the following fact, implicit in (
5.36
) and clarified in Lemma
5.8
:

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{1},{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}\in\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}},

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{l}}_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{2})\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\left(\nexists C\in{\mathcal{C}}\;\;|\;\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{1},{\mathfrak{l}}_{2}\in C\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.37)

We now intent to introduce our central ontic notion.

Definition 5.10
.

Once is known the compatibility cover
{\mathcal{C}}
, we define an
operational description

{\mathfrak{D}}
to be a family of maps
({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
, where each map
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}
is an element of
{\mathfrak{B}}^{C}
(i.e. a counterfactual statement in
{\mathfrak{B}}
is attributed to any of the jointly compatible measurements
{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}}
for any
{\mathfrak{l}}\in C
), these maps being assigned to satisfy the following conditions

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}})=\textit{\bf Y}

(5.38)

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}(\overline{\;\mathfrak{l}\;})=\overline{{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})},

(5.39)

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{i}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq C,\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{i\in I}{\mathfrak{l}}_{i})=\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{i}).

(5.40)

Definition 5.11
.

If the operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}:=({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfies the following supplementary condition

(sheaf condition)

\displaystyle\forall C,C^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\cap C^{\prime},\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{\prime}}({\mathfrak{l}}),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.41)

it will be designated as a
coherent operational description
.

The set of coherent operational descriptions associated to the compatibility cover
{\mathcal{C}}
will be denoted
{\mathcal{D}}
and called
the empirical model
for the space of states
{\mathfrak{S}}
.

Definition 5.12
.

The empirical model
{\mathcal{D}}
associated to the space of states
{\mathfrak{S}}
is an Inf semi-lattice by defining its infima as follows :

\displaystyle\forall({\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}},({\mathfrak{d}}_{2}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in{\mathcal{D}},\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{C}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathcal{D}}}}{\mathfrak{d}}_{2}^{C})({\mathfrak{l}}):={\mathfrak{d}}_{1}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}_{2}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.42)

Definition 5.13
.

If an operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}:=({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
is such that there exists a real state
\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\sigma),

(5.43)

we will say that
{\mathfrak{D}}
is
defined globally
. It will then be denoted
({\mathfrak{d}}^{C}_{(\sigma)})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
. Note that if
{\mathfrak{D}}
is defined globally, then it is trivially a coherent operational description.

Definition 5.14
.

The empirical model
{\mathcal{D}}
is said to be
non-contextual
iff every
{\mathfrak{D}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
is defined globally, otherwise it is said to be
contextual
.

Theorem 5.15
.

If
{\mathfrak{S}}
is a simplex then every coherent operational description
{\mathfrak{d}}
are defined globally. The empirical model associated to a simplex space of states is then non-contextual.

Proof.

Direct consequence of Theorem
5.4
and Theorem
2.15
.
∎

It appears clearly that an operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}
defines a collection of preparation procedures. More explicitly, each complete context
U
leads to the preparation procedure selecting the samples which return the correct result (i.e. the correct counterfactual statement) to the family of jointly-compatible measurements
({\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}})_{{\mathfrak{l}}\in U}
.

In an empirical model, the aim of a preparation procedure is to select an ontic state for the system (or at least a mixture of individual ontic states) compatible with all the observed data. The knowledge of this ontic state is necessary and exactly sufficient to fix the result returned by any of the measurements that can be realized simultaneously (i.e. in a jointly-compatible way) on the system.

Definition 5.16
.

The
ontic state
associated to the operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}:=({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in{\mathcal{D}}
is a family
(\Sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfying the following conditions

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}}),

(5.44)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\Sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\mathfrak{S}}}\{\,\sigma^{C}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;|\;\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;{\mathfrak{m}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\sigma^{C})\geq{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})\,\}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.45)

Theorem 5.17
.

Let us fix a coherent operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}:=({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in{{\mathcal{D}}}
associated to the compatibility cover
{\mathcal{C}}
. Let us also fix a compatibility context
C
. Then,

\displaystyle\exists\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle(\,\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\sigma)={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}}),\;\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\,).

(5.46)

More explicitly, we will fix explicitly the solution
\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
of the equation (
5.46
) defined by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\;|\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})=\textit{\bf Y}\,\}

(5.47)

\displaystyle\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}})^{-1}(\textit{\bf Y})\;\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}

(5.48)

The family
(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{\,\mathcal{C}}
is the ontic state associated to
{\mathfrak{D}}
.

Proof.

Let us consider the effect
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}:=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\;|\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})=\textit{\bf Y}\,\}
. Note that the infimum in the definition of
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
exists because
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
is a down-complete Inf semi-lattice and because
{\mathfrak{Y}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\in\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\;|\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})=\textit{\bf Y}\,\}\not=\varnothing
. Moreover, due to the property (
5.36
), we deduce that
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
is in
C
.

Secondly,
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}
because of the property (
5.40
).

Thirdly, we note that, for any
{\mathfrak{l}}\in C
, we have
{\mathfrak{l}}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
implies
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\textit{\bf Y}
because
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})\wedge\textit{\bf Y}={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}
. Conversely, the property
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\textit{\bf Y}
for
{\mathfrak{l}}\in C
implies
{\mathfrak{l}}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
from the explicit definition of
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
.

As a result, we have
\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}\in C\;|\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})=\textit{\bf Y}\,\}=(\uparrow^{{}^{C}}\!\!{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})
.

We also note that, due to properties (
5.38
) and the definition of
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
, we have
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}={\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{E}}}}{{\mathfrak{Y}}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}}
. Hence, there must exist a state
\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}\in{\mathfrak{S}}
such that
{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}={\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}},\,\cdot\,)}
. In other words,
\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}:=\bigsqcap^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}})^{-1}(\textit{\bf Y})
.

We can summarize these results by the following equivalence :

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})=\textit{\bf Y}\;\Leftrightarrow\;\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}.

(5.49)

Using (
2.40
) and (
5.39
), we deduce also :
\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf N}\;\Leftrightarrow\;\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\overline{\,\mathfrak{l}\,}}(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}\;\Leftrightarrow\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}(\overline{\,{\mathfrak{l}}\,})=\textit{\bf Y}\;\Leftrightarrow\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\textit{\bf N}
.
As a final conclusion, we have for any
{\mathfrak{l}}\in C
the equality
\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})
.

Note then that the defining property (
5.44
) is identical to the property (
5.46
) satisfied by
\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
for any
C\in{\mathcal{C}}
.

Let us now consider a family
(\rho^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{{\mathcal{C}}}
satisfying
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\rho^{C})\geq{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({{\mathfrak{l}}})
.

We have then in particular
\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}}(\rho^{C})\geq{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}
. Then
\rho^{C}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}})^{-1}(\textit{\bf Y})=\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}}
. This is the defining property (
5.45
).

As a conclusion,
(\sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
is the ontic state associated to
{\mathfrak{D}}
.
∎

5.2.
Hidden states and contextuality

During this subsection,
(\!({\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime},\star)\!)_{c}:=({\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}({\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}),cl_{c}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}}},\star)
will define the ontic completion of
({\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime},\star)
. We will denote equivalently by
{\mathfrak{S}}
the space of states
{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}}}
, and we will then denote by
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
the space of states
{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}
. As shown before, the states/effects Chu Space
({\mathfrak{S}},{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}}},\epsilon^{{\mathfrak{S}}})
is equipped with a real structure given simply by
(\overline{\mathfrak{S}},\star)
.

As before,
\overline{\mathfrak{E}}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}
is defined as the sub Inf semi-lattice of
{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}
generated by the elements of

\displaystyle\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}\;|\;\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\},\sigma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma^{\star}\,\}\cup\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}}\,\}

Definition 5.18
.

In the following, we will adopt the following notation :

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\left(\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{{}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}}\,\}\right).

(5.50)

Let us consider a maximal compatibility context
C
in
{\mathcal{C}}
. We are necessarily in one of the two following cases :

•

Maximal compatibility context of Type 1 :

\displaystyle C

\displaystyle\subseteq

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}

(5.51)

•

Maximal compatibility context of Type 2 :

\displaystyle\exists\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\},\sigma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma^{\star}

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\sigma^{\prime})}\in C.

(5.52)

Lemma 5.19
.

Let us consider a compatibility context denoted
C
such that
C\subseteq{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}
. We have necessarily

\displaystyle\exists\,\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\;|\;\;C\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\right),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.53)

or

\displaystyle\exists\,\gamma,\gamma^{\prime},\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\;|\;\;\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star},\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma,\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\prime}

\displaystyle C\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\gamma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\gamma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\cup\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\gamma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\right)\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.54)

Proof.

Let us consider a compatibility context denoted
C
such that
C\subseteq{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}
and let us consider the following subset of
{\mathcal{P}}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{Q}}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\,U\subseteq C\;|\;\forall\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;\;\textit{\rm s.t.}\;\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)},{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma^{\prime},\centerdot)}\in U\;\;\textit{\rm we have}\;\;\sigma^{\prime}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}}}\sigma^{\star}\,\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.55)

and let us consider an element
V
which is maximal for inclusion in
{\mathfrak{Q}}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
. As a trivial consequence of the requirement (
5.2
) (more explicitly, of the property (
5.29
)), we know that
\omega:=\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\sigma\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in V\}
exists in
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
.

If we denote
C_{\omega}:=\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\right)
, we note that
C_{\omega}
is a compatibility context (the joint morphism is built straightforwardly) and
V\subseteq C_{\omega}
by construction.

We will now distinguish two cases : (1)
C\smallsetminus V=\varnothing
, and (2)
C\smallsetminus V\not=\varnothing
.

The first case is treated immediately, we obtain (
5.53
).

Let us then focus on the second case. For any
\alpha\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
such that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}\in C\smallsetminus V
, we have necessarily, due to the requirement (
5.2
) (more explicitly, of the property (
5.29
)), for any
\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
such that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in V
the property
\alpha^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma
, i.e.
\alpha^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in V\,\}
. If we denote by
\gamma:=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in V\,\}
and
\gamma^{\prime}:=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in C\smallsetminus V\,\}
, we have
\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}
. On another part, and for the same reasons as before, if we denote
\omega^{\prime}:=\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\{\,\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;|\;{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\in C\smallsetminus V\,\}
, then
C_{\omega^{\prime}}
is a compatibility context (the joint morphism is built straightforwardly) and
(C\smallsetminus V)\subseteq C_{\omega^{\prime}}
by construction.

This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.20
.

In the following, we will adopt the following notation

\displaystyle\forall\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\;\;|\;\;C^{(1)}_{\omega}:=\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.56)

Let
C
be a maximal compatibility context of type 1. Then,

\displaystyle\exists\textit{\bf!}\,\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\;\;|\;\;C=C^{(1)}_{\omega}.

(5.57)

Proof.

Trivial consequence of Lemma
5.19
.
∎

Lemma 5.21
.

Let
C
be a maximal compatibility context of type 2 and let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
with
\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}
and such that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\in C
. Then,

\displaystyle\exists\,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;\;|\;\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\star},\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda,\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\prime}

\displaystyle(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\cup\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\right)

(5.58)

Proof.

Let
C
be a maximal compatibility context of type 2 and let
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
with
\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}
and such that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\in C
.

Let us first note that
(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})
is a compatibility context included in
{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Secondly, we remark that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)},{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma^{\prime},\centerdot)}\in(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})
. Indeed, we recall that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)}={\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma^{\prime},\centerdot)}=\overline{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\prime})}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
and we have Theorem
5.9
for the maximal compatibility context
C
. Moreover, we have
\neg\widehat{\gamma\gamma^{\prime}}{}^{{}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}
. We are then in the case (
5.54
) and not in the case (
5.53
). Because of Lemma
5.19
, we know that

\displaystyle\exists\,\lambda,\lambda^{\prime},\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;\;|\;\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\star},\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda,\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\prime}

\displaystyle(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\cup\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\right)

(5.59)

and we must obviously have
\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma
and
\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\prime}
, because
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)},{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma^{\prime},\centerdot)}\in C
.
