∎

Lemma 5.22
.

Let
C
be a maximal compatibility context of type 2 such that there exists
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
with
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})}\in C
. Then,

\displaystyle\exists\,\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}\;\;|\;\;\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma,\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}

\displaystyle(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\gamma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\gamma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\cup\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\left.\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\gamma^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\gamma^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\right)

(5.60)

Proof.

We obviously take Lemma
5.21
as a departure point.

If
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma^{\star}
we have then
\gamma^{\star}=\gamma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\star}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}
.
This concludes the proof.
∎

In the following,
{\mathcal{C}}
will designate the compatibility cover and we will consider a coherent operational description denoted
{\mathfrak{D}}:=({\mathfrak{d}}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
. We will denote by
(\Sigma^{C}_{\mathfrak{D}})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
the corresponding ontic state.

Lemma 5.23
.

For any pair
C^{(1)}_{\omega},C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}
of maximal compatibility contexts of type 1 associated to
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}
(see Lemma
5.20
), we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{rcl}\omega\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}&\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}&\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\\
\omega^{\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}&\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}&\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\end{array}\right.

Proof.

By definition of the ontic state, we have

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C^{(1)}_{\omega},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})

(5.64)

The consistency of
{\mathfrak{D}}
implies

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C^{(1)}_{\omega}\cap C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}},

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}})

(5.65)

But,

\displaystyle C^{(1)}_{\omega}\cap C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.66)

Hence,

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left(\sigma\in\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\right)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\left(\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}})\right)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}.\;\;\;\;\;

(5.67)

In other words, noting that
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega
, we obtain
\omega^{\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
. The other inequality is obtained in the same way.
∎

Lemma 5.24
.

If there exists
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
and
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
admit a common upper-bound in
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
, then for any
\omega^{\prime\prime}\in\underline{\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqcup_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}}_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}
we have
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqcup_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}})

Proof.

Straightforward.
∎

Lemma 5.25
.

The subset of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
defined by
\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}
satisfies

\displaystyle\nexists\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\{\,\gamma,\gamma^{\star}\,\}\sqsubseteq\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}.

(5.68)

Proof.

Let us suppose that there exists
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
such that
\{\,\gamma,\gamma^{\star}\,\}\sqsubseteq\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\sigma}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}
, and let us exhibit a contradiction. We denote by
\omega
and
\omega^{\prime}
the elements of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\gamma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
and
\gamma^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
. There exists a maximal compatibility context of type 2 denoted
C
such that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})}\in C
. Applying Theorem
5.9
to the maximal context
C
we deduce easily that
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)}\in C
and
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})}\in C
. Due to the sheaf condition satisfied by
{\mathfrak{D}}
, we know that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}

(5.69)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf N}

(5.70)

but we have also

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\centerdot)})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})})=\textit{\bf Y}\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})})

(5.71)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})})={\mathfrak{d}}^{C}(\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})})=\textit{\bf N}\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})}).

(5.72)

From (
5.69
) and (
5.71
) we deduce that necessarily
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\gamma,\gamma^{\star})})=\textit{\bf Y}
, but using (
5.72
) we deduce
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\gamma^{\star})})=\textit{\bf N}\wedge\textit{\bf Y}=\bot
which contradicts (
5.70
). We have then obtained the announced contradiction. This concludes the proof.
∎

Definition 5.26
.

We will say that the operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}
is
admissible
iff
\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}
is admissible, i.e. iff
\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
. The set of admissible operational description will be denoted
{\mathcal{D}}_{{}_{Adm}}
.

Lemma 5.27
.

As long as the operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}
is admissible, the supremum

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}

(5.73)

is well defined as an element of
{\mathfrak{S}}
.
Then,we have

\displaystyle\forall\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}},

\displaystyle\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}=\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}

(5.74)

Proof.

Using the assumption requiring
({\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime},\star)
to admit an ontic completion and the fact that
{\mathfrak{D}}
is admissible, we deduce that

\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\}
is in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{c}(\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
and, then,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}}}(\{\,\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\;|\;\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}\,\})

(5.75)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}

(5.76)

is well defined as an element of
{\mathfrak{S}}
.

Then,
\forall\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}},\;\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
implies
\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
.

On another part,
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}
implies
\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}
.

Endly, we recall that
\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}=\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
.

As a conclusion, we obtain
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}=\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}
.

Conversely, we easily check that the expression (
5.74
) satisfies the constraints (
5.23
).
∎

Lemma 5.28
.

Let us consider a maximal compatibility context of type 1 denoted
C^{(1)}_{\omega}
. We have

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C^{(1)}_{\omega},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

(5.77)

Proof.

Let us compute
{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}})
for any
{\mathfrak{l}}\in C^{(1)}_{\omega}
. First of all, we note that, for any
\alpha\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\omega
, we have

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\sqcap_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}\wedge\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

(5.78)

and then

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle\in

\displaystyle\{\textit{\bf Y},\bot\}.

(5.79)

As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\omega\sqcap_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})=\textit{\bf Y}\wedge\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.80)

We can check analogously

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\alpha)})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\alpha)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

(5.81)

∎

Lemma 5.29
.

Let us consider a maximal compatibility context of type 2 denoted by
C
. We have

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

(5.82)

Proof.

Let us consider a maximal compatibility context of type 2 denoted by
C
and let us choose
\lambda,\lambda^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\}
such that
\lambda^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\star}
and
\omega,\omega^{\prime}\in{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda,\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\prime}
with

\displaystyle(C\cap{\mathfrak{U}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}})\subseteq\left(\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\centerdot)}\,\}\cup\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left.\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\sigma,\centerdot)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\cup\{\,{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\sigma)}\;|\;\lambda^{\prime}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\prime}\,\}\right)

(5.83)

We note that

\displaystyle\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}\in C,

\displaystyle\left(\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\omega^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda^{\prime}\right).

(5.84)

We also note that, for any
{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}\in C
, we have

\displaystyle\textit{\bf Y}\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})})

(5.85)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\centerdot)}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textit{\bf Y}\wedge\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

and

\displaystyle\textit{\bf N}\wedge{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}(\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})})

(5.86)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\alpha^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C^{(1)}_{\omega^{\prime}}}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\alpha^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\centerdot,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{Y}}_{{\mathfrak{E}}_{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\textit{\bf N}\wedge\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

From these two relations, we deduce immediately

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{(\alpha,\alpha^{\prime})}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

(5.87)

∎

Theorem 5.30
.

Let us consider an admissible operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}
. We have then necessarily the following property :

\displaystyle\exists\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}:=(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.88)

Proof.

Direct consequence of Lemma
5.27
,Lemma
5.28
,Lemma
5.29
.
∎

Theorem 5.31
.

The map from
{\mathcal{D}}_{{}_{Adm}}
to
{\mathfrak{S}}
, sending
{\mathfrak{D}}
to
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}:=(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})
is an isomorphism.

Proof.

First of all, we note that we can effectively associate to any
{\mathfrak{D}}\in{\mathcal{D}}_{{}_{Adm}}
an element
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}).

(5.89)

This point is guarantied by Lemma
5.27
, Lemma
5.28
and Lemma
5.29
. More explicitly, we have
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}:=(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}})\in{\mathfrak{S}}
where the state
\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}}
is defined in reference to (
5.47
) (
5.48
) and Lemma
5.20
.

Note that the supremum defining
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}
exists in
{\mathfrak{S}}
because
{\mathfrak{D}}
is admissible.

Secondly, this map is clearly injective. Indeed, let us consider a pair
{\mathfrak{D}},{\mathfrak{D}}^{\prime}
of coherent operational descriptions such that
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}=\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}
. We have obviously
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\;\;{\mathfrak{d}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}})={\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}})
.

Endly, this map is also clearly a surjection. Indeed, we can associate, to any
\alpha\in{\mathfrak{S}}
, a coherent operational description
{\mathfrak{D}}_{\alpha}:=({\mathfrak{d}}_{(\alpha)}^{C})_{C\in{\mathcal{C}}}
by
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,{\mathfrak{d}}_{(\alpha)}^{C}({\mathfrak{l}}):=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\alpha)
. We have then, for any
\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}
,

\displaystyle\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega.

(5.90)

We then build as usual
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}}:=(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}}}_{\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}^{{}^{pure}}}\;\Sigma^{C^{(1)}_{\omega}}_{\mathfrak{D}_{\alpha}})
. We have by construction
\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{(\alpha)}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\alpha)
. Using (
2.82
), we then conclude that
\Sigma_{{\mathfrak{D}}_{(\alpha)}}=\alpha
.

Endly, we derive easily the homomorphic property from the properties of the evaluation map
\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}
:

\displaystyle\forall C\in{\mathcal{C}},\forall{\mathfrak{l}}\in C,

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{d}}^{C}\sqcap_{{}_{\mathcal{D}}}{\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}}^{C})({\mathfrak{l}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}})\wedge\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}})=\epsilon^{\mathfrak{S}}_{\mathfrak{l}}(\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\Sigma_{\mathfrak{D}^{\prime}}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(5.91)

∎

6.
Bipartite indeterministic experiments

The purpose of this section is to continue our work on constructing the tensor product of spaces of states. This time, we will focus on indeterministic spaces of states (the Section
4
has already dealt with the case of deterministic spaces of states). We will use the results from Sections
3

and
4
to produce a definition of their tensor product.

6.1.
Complementary results about the minimal tensor product

During this subsection, we consider the two ontic completions
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},\star)\!)_{c}
and
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)\!)_{c}
.

The aim of this introductory subsection is to prove that
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}:=\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
fulfills the basic conditions necessary to consider its ”ontic completion”. We recall that the basic conditions necessary for
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
to admit an ontic completion are

(1)

{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
has to be a down complete Inf semi-lattice with bottom element
\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}
, such that
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
is generated by its maximal elements, i.e.

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB},\;\;\sigma=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\;\;\textit{\rm where}\;\;\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}:=\{\,\sigma^{\prime}\in{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{\,{}^{pure}}\;|\;\sigma^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma\;\}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{\,{}^{pure}}:=Max({{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB})\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.1)

(2)

\star
has to be a map from
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\}
to
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\}
satisfying

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\},

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\star})^{\star}=\sigma,

(6.2)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\},

\displaystyle\sigma_{1}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\sigma_{2}\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\sigma_{2}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\sigma_{1}^{\star},

(6.3)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\},

\displaystyle\neg\;\widehat{\sigma^{\star}\sigma}{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}.

(6.4)

We have already proven, in subsection
4.1
, that
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
is a down complete Inf semi-lattice and that it admits a bottom element (Theorem
4.10
).
Let us then prove that
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
is generated by its pure states.

We begin first to characterize the maximal states of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}=\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
.

Theorem 6.1
.

\displaystyle{{{\overline{S}}}}{}^{\;{}^{pure}}_{AB}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{\;{}^{pure}}_{A},\sigma_{B}\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{\;{}^{pure}}_{B}\,\}=Max({{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB})

(6.5)

Proof.

First of all, it is a tautological fact that the completely meet-irreducible elements of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
are necessarily pure tensors of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
, i.e. elements of the form
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
.

Let us then consider
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
a completely meet-irreducible element of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
and let us assume that
\sigma_{A}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}
for
\sigma_{i,A}\in{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
for any
i\in I
. We have then
(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})=((\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A})\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})
. On another part,
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
being completely meet-irreducible in
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
, there exists
k\in I
such that
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}=\sigma_{k,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
, i.e,
\sigma_{A}=\sigma_{k,A}
. As a conclusion,
\sigma_{A}
is completely meet-irreducible. In the same way,
\sigma_{B}
is completely meet-irreducible. As a first result, pure states of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
are necessarily of the form
\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}
with
\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{\;{}^{pure}}_{A},\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{\;{}^{pure}}_{B}
.

Conversely, let us consider
\sigma_{A}
a pure state of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}
a pure state of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
, and let us suppose that
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})=(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})
with
\sigma_{i,A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\sigma_{i,B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
for any
i\in I
. We now exploit the two conditions
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in I}\;\sigma_{k,A})=\sigma_{A}
and
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I}\;\sigma_{m,B})=\sigma_{B}
derived from the expansion (
4.24
). From
\sigma_{A}\in Max(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A})
and
\sigma_{B}\in Max(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B})
, we deduce that
\sigma_{i,A}=\sigma_{A}
and
\sigma_{j,B}=\sigma_{B}
for any
i,j\in I
. As a second result, we have then obtained that the state
(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})
, with
\sigma_{A}
a pure state of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}
a pure state of
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
, is completely meet-irreducible.

From the expansion (
4.24
), we deduce also immediately that
(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})\in Max({{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB})
as long as
\sigma_{A}\in Max(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A})
and
\sigma_{B}\in Max(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B})
.
∎

Secondly, we intent to prove that these pure states of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
form a generating set for
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
.

Theorem 6.2
.

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB},\;\;\sigma=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}},\;\;\textit{\rm where}\;\;\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}=({{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{\,{}^{pure}}\cap(\uparrow^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!\!\sigma)).

(6.6)

Proof.

Let us fix
\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
.

We note that
\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma^{\prime}
for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in({{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{\,{}^{pure}}\cap(\uparrow^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!\!\sigma))
and then
\sigma\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}
.

Secondly, denoting

\sigma:=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})
, we note immediately that, for any

\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{pure}
and
\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{pure}
, if

\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{i,A}
and
\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{i,B}
, then
(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{AB}}}\sigma
, i.e.
(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})\in\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}
. As a consequence, we have

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{i,A}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{i,B}}\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.7)

Endly, we have

\displaystyle\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{i,A}}\sigma_{A})\widetilde{\otimes}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{i,B}}\sigma_{B})

(6.8)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{i,A}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{i,B}}\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}.

As a final conclusion, we obtain

\displaystyle\sigma=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{A}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{i,A}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\sigma_{B}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{pure}\;|\;\sigma_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{i,B}}\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\underline{\sigma}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.9)

∎

We have then completed the first group of conditions about
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
.

By the way, we can exploit the previous result to produce a formula for the suprema of elements of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
.

Theorem 6.3
.

Let
\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}
be two elements of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
having a common upper-bound. Then the supremum of
\{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB},\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}\}
exists in
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
and its expression is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in(\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\cap\;\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!)}\;\widetilde{\sigma}

(6.10)

Proof.

As long as
\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}
have a common upper-bound,
\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}\cap\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}
is not empty. Secondly, it is clear that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}\cap\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}\cap\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma}
. Then, if we suppose there exists
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}
such that
\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB},\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}
we can use Theorem
6.2
to obtain the decomposition
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma})
with necessarily
\forall\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}},

\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}
and
\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}
, i.e.
\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}\cap\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}
, and then
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{\widetilde{\sigma}\in\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}_{AB}}\cap\underline{\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime}_{AB}}}\;\widetilde{\sigma})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\widetilde{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}_{AB}
. ∎

We now intent to show that
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
fulfills the second group of conditions necessary to build its ”ontic completion” (the conditions on the star map).

Let us first establish a result about atomicity of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
. We recall that
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
are both atomic.

Lemma 6.4
.

\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
being atomic, then
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
is also atomic, i.e.

\displaystyle\exists{\mathcal{A}}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}\subseteq{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\forall\alpha_{AB}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}},\;\;(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\alpha_{AB},

(6.12)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{AB}\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB},\;\;\exists\alpha_{AB}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}\;|\;\alpha_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{AB}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

The set of atoms of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
is indeed defined by

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\,(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})\;|\;\alpha_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\;\alpha_{B}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\,\}.

(6.13)

Proof.

Using the expansion (
4.24
), we deduce immediately

\displaystyle\forall\alpha_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\forall\alpha_{B}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},

\displaystyle(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})\;\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\;\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.14)

In other words,
\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqsubset_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})
.

Secondly, let us show that, for any
\sigma_{AB}:=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})
distinct from
\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
, there exist
\alpha_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\alpha_{B}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
such that
((\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}))\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{AB}
. Using once again the expansion (
4.24
), we know that
\sigma_{AB}\sqsupset_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
(or, in other words,
\sigma_{AB}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
) implies that there exists
\varnothing\subseteq K\subseteq I
such that
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsupset_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsupset_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
. Let us fix such a
K
and let us choose
\alpha_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\alpha_{B}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
such that
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha_{A}
and
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\alpha_{B}
. We obtain
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in K}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})\;\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})
and
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I-K}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})\;\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})
. As a first conclusion, we obtain
((\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}))\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{AB}
.

Thirdly, let us consider
\sigma_{AB}:=(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B})
such that
\sigma_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})
. As a first case, we may have obviously
\sigma_{AB}=\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
. If however
\sigma_{AB}\not=\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
, the previous result implies that there exist
\alpha^{\prime}_{A}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\alpha^{\prime}_{B}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
such that
((\alpha^{\prime}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha^{\prime}_{B}))\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{AB}
. Using once again the expansion (
4.24
), we deduce immediately that
\alpha_{A}=\alpha^{\prime}_{A}
and
\alpha_{B}=\alpha^{\prime}_{B}
. As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\sigma_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left(\sigma_{AB}=\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\sigma_{AB}=(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.15)

As a second conclusion, we then obtain
\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B})
.
∎

Definition 6.5
.

We define the map
\star
from
{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\}
to itself as follows.

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}},\beta\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}},

\displaystyle(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}:=\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}

(6.16)

\displaystyle\forall\sigma\in{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\},

\displaystyle\sigma^{\star}:=\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}_{\omega\;\in\;\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}}\omega^{\star}.

(6.17)

Let
\sigma
be an element of
{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}
.

Theorem 6.6
.

The map
\star
is involutive and order-reversing and satisfies
\forall\sigma\in{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\},\;\;\;\;\neg\;\widehat{\sigma^{\star}\sigma}{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}
.

Proof.

First of all, the order-reversing property is easy to derive. Indeed,
\sigma\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\sigma^{\prime}
implies
\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\subseteq\underline{\,\sigma^{\prime}\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}
, and then implies
\sigma^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\sigma^{\prime}{}^{\star}
.

Let us now consider
((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star})^{\star}
for
\alpha\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}},\beta\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
. Due to the order-reversing property and the fact that
(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}
is an atom, we know that
((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star})^{\star}
is an element of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}^{\;{}^{pure}}
. A direct computation shows that
((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star})^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
. Indeed, for any
\omega\in\underline{\alpha^{\star}}_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and any
\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
, we have
\omega^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega^{\prime}{}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
. And for any
\omega\in\underline{\beta^{\star}}_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
and any
\omega^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}
, we have
\omega^{\prime}{}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega{}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
. Using these two results, we obtain
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
. As a first conclusion, we then obtain
((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star})^{\star}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta

Now, using the order-reversing property, we deduce

\displaystyle(\sigma^{\star})^{\star}:=(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}_{\omega\;\in\;\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}}\omega^{\star})^{\star}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}_{\omega\;\in\;\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}}(\omega^{\star})^{\star}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}_{\omega\;\in\;\underline{\,\sigma\,}_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}}\omega=\sigma

(6.18)

The property
\forall\sigma\in{\mathfrak{S}}^{\prime}\smallsetminus\{\bot_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}}\},\neg\;\widehat{\sigma^{\star}\sigma}{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}^{\prime}}}}
is also required for the map
\star
. This property is easy to check using the formula (
4.24
). Indeed, for any
\{\,(\sigma_{i,A},\sigma_{i,B})\;|\;i\in I\}\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}\times\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
and any
(\sigma_{A},\sigma_{B})\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}\times\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
, we have that

\displaystyle\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{B}

(6.19)

is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left((\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in I}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\forall\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I,\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}\right)\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.20)

On another part,

\displaystyle\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)^{\star}=\left(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}^{\star})\right)\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma_{B}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.21)

is equivalent to

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left((\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in I}\;\sigma_{k,A}^{\star})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I}\;\sigma_{m,B}^{\star})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left(\exists\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\varsubsetneq I,\;\;(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A}^{\star})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{m\in I-K}\;\sigma_{m,B}^{\star})\;\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}\right)\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.22)

As a conclusion, we obtain that either there exists
\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\subseteq I
such that
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A})
and
(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,A}^{\star})
admit
\sigma_{A}
as their common upper bound, or there exists
\varnothing\varsubsetneq K\subseteq I
such that
(\bigsqcap{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,B})
and
(\bigsqcup{}^{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{k\in K}\;\sigma_{k,B}^{\star})
admit
\sigma_{B}
as their common upper bound. We have then obtained a contradiction. Hence,
\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)
and
\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)^{\star}
do not admit any common upper bound in
{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{\,{}^{pure}}
.
∎

We have then completed the check of the second condition necessary to build the ”ontic completion” of
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
.

We take the occasion of this introductory subsection to prove two complementary results.

Theorem 6.7
.

Let us suppose that
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
both satisfy the following basic property

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\forall\alpha,\beta\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},

\displaystyle\beta\not=\alpha\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha,\beta.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.23)

Then
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
satisfies the same property.

In other words, for any
\sigma,\omega\in{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\omega\not=\sigma
we have then
(\omega\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\sigma,\omega
.

Proof.

Trivial consequence of the expansion formula (
4.24
).
∎

Theorem 6.8
.

Let us suppose that
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}
and
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
both satisfy the following basic property

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\forall\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;\textit{\rm distinct},

\displaystyle(\,(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta)\not=(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\delta)\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}\lambda\,)\;\;\Rightarrow

(6.24)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\delta).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

Then
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}
satisfies the same property.

In other words, if we consider
\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{{}^{pure}}
distinct, such that
(\,(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta)\not=(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)
and
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\lambda\,)
, we have then
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of the expansion formula (
4.24
). Indeed,
\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}^{{}^{pure}}
all distinct with
(\,(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta)\not=(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)
and
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}}}}\lambda\,)
, imply that we are in one of the following cases (we denote
\lambda:=\sigma_{\lambda}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\lambda}
,
\alpha:=\sigma_{\alpha}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\alpha}
,
\beta:=\sigma_{\beta}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\beta}
,
\gamma:=\sigma_{\gamma}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\gamma}
,
\delta:=\sigma_{\delta}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\delta}
with
\sigma_{\lambda},\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta},\sigma_{\gamma},\sigma_{\delta}\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}
,
\kappa_{\lambda},\sigma_{\alpha},\sigma_{\beta},\sigma_{\gamma},\sigma_{\delta}\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
):

(1)
\kappa_{\alpha}=\kappa_{\beta}=\kappa_{\lambda}
and
\kappa_{\gamma}=\kappa_{\delta}=\kappa_{\lambda}
and
(\sigma_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\beta}),(\sigma_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\delta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\lambda}
and
(\sigma_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\beta})\not=(\sigma_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\delta})
.

(2)
\sigma_{\alpha}=\sigma_{\beta}=\sigma_{\lambda}
and
\sigma_{\gamma}=\sigma_{\delta}=\sigma_{\lambda}
and
(\kappa_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\beta}),(\kappa_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\delta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\kappa_{\lambda}
and
(\kappa_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\beta})\not=(\kappa_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\delta})
.

(3)
\sigma_{\alpha}=\sigma_{\beta}=\sigma_{\lambda}
and
\kappa_{\gamma}=\kappa_{\delta}=\kappa_{\lambda}
and
(\kappa_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\beta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\kappa_{\lambda}
and
(\sigma_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\delta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{\lambda}
.

(4)
\kappa_{\alpha}=\kappa_{\beta}=\kappa_{\lambda}
and
\sigma_{\gamma}=\sigma_{\delta}=\sigma_{\lambda}
and
(\sigma_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\beta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{\lambda}
and
(\kappa_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\delta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\kappa_{\lambda}
.

In the case (1) we then apply the property (
6.24
) satisfied by
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}_{A}
to conclude

In the case (2) we then apply the property (
6.24
) satisfied by
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}_{B}
to conclude.

In the case (3) we just observe that
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\delta)=(\sigma_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\delta})\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{\lambda}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{S}_{AB}}}\sigma_{\lambda}\widetilde{\otimes}(\kappa_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{B}}}}\kappa_{\beta})
and we recall that

(\kappa_{\alpha}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\kappa_{\beta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\kappa_{\lambda}
and
(\sigma_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}_{A}}}}\sigma_{\delta})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{\lambda}
to conclude.

The case (4) is similar to the case (3).
∎

We are now ready to try to define the tensor product of two ontic completions.

6.2.
The fundamental description of bipartite indeterministic experiments

During this subsection, we consider two ontic completions
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},\star)\!)_{c}
and
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)\!)_{c}
. As usual we denote

{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
.

We now intent to expose a first natural proposal for the description of the bipartite experiments associated to completely indeterministic compound systems (we will consider these compound systems as being built from two systems which separate descriptions are ensured by the ontic completions
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},\star)\!)_{c}
and
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)\!)_{c}
).

Definition 6.9
.

We define the tensor product of
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
denoted
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
by

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\;\widehat{\otimes}\;{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}

(6.25)

We note that the ontic completion of
(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)
can be defined because the basic conditions are fulfilled.

Our aim is now to check all the basic requirements addressed in subsection
4.1
.

Lemma 6.10
.

{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
is a down complete Inf semi-lattice.
The lowest bound is given by
\bot_{{}_{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}=\bot_{{}_{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of the construction of the ontic completion
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)\!)_{c}
.
∎

Lemma 6.11
.

{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
admits a real structure given by
({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of Theorem
3.21
for
{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
defined as the ontic completion of
({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
.
∎

Lemma 6.12
.

There exists a map
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
from
{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{\times}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to
{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
. This map is simply given by

\displaystyle\forall U_{A}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\forall U_{B}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},

\displaystyle\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B}):=\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.26)

Proof.

Let us introduce a map denoted
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
given by

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall U_{A}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\forall U_{B}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},

\displaystyle\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B}):=cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\left(\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.27)

By construction
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B})
is closed. In order to prove that
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B})
is an element of
{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
, we have to prove also that
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B})\in{\mathcal{K}}({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
. In order to prove this fundamental property, we first note that the expression of
\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B})
can be simplified.

First of all, we have, for any
U_{A}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}})
and
U_{B}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
,

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptcl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\left(\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\right)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\{\,\omega_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega_{B}\;|\;\omega_{A}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{A}),\omega_{B}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{B})\,\}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.28)

To prove this intermediary result, we begin with the following obvious inequality

\displaystyle\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}U_{A})\widetilde{\otimes}(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}U_{B}),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.29)

Now, we use the monotonicity of
\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to deduce

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptcl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\left(\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\right)\sqsubseteq\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\left((\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}U_{A})\widetilde{\otimes}(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}U_{B})\right).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.30)

We then recall that

\displaystyle\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}(u_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}u_{B})=Max(\{\,v_{AB}\in{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\;|\;v_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}u_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}u_{B}\,\})

(6.31)

and
v_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}u_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}u_{B}
imposes the existence of
u^{\prime}_{A}\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(u_{A})
and
u^{\prime}_{B}\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}(u_{B})
such that
v_{AB}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}u^{\prime}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}u^{\prime}_{B}
. As a result, we obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\left((\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}U_{A})\widetilde{\otimes}(\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}U_{B})\right)\sqsubseteq\{\,\omega_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega_{B}\;|\;\omega_{A}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{A}),\omega_{B}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{B})\,\}.\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.32)

Now we use the intermediary result (
6.28
) with the properties of the closure
cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
and the properties
U_{A}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
U_{B}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
to obtain

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\left(\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\right)\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.33)

and

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptcl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\left(\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\right)

\displaystyle\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle\{\,\omega_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega_{B}\;|\;\omega_{A}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{A}),\omega_{B}\in cl_{c}^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}(U_{B})\,\}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.34)

Hence,

\displaystyle\iota^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(U_{A},U_{B})

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}.

(6.35)

Let us come back to the main proof : we have to check that
\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\in{\mathcal{K}}({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
. Let us then consider
\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}
,
\{\,\sigma^{\prime}_{k,A}\;|\;k\in K\,\}\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}
,

\{\,\sigma_{j,B}\;|\;j\in J\,\}\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
,
\{\,\sigma^{\prime}_{l,B}\;|\;l\in L\,\}\subseteq\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\right)\widetilde{\otimes}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma_{j,B}\right)\right)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\left(\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\sigma_{k,A}\right)\widetilde{\otimes}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{l\in L}\sigma_{l,B}\right)\right)

(6.36)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\bigsqcup{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{j\in J}\left(\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{j,B}\right)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{l\in L}\left(\sigma_{k,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{l,B}\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\forall i\in I,j\in J,k\in K,l\in L,\;\left(\left(\sigma_{i,A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\right)\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\left(\bot_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{j,B}^{\star}\right)\right)\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\left(\sigma_{k,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{l,B}\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\forall i\in I,j\in J,k\in K,l\in L,\;\left(\sigma_{i,A}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{k,A}\;\textit{\rm or}\;\sigma_{j,B}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{l,B}\right).

In other words,

\displaystyle\left(\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\right)\widetilde{\otimes}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma_{j,B}\right)\right)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\left(\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\sigma_{k,A}\right)\widetilde{\otimes}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{l\in L}\sigma_{l,B}\right)\right)

(6.37)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\exists i\in I,j\in J,k\in K,l\in L,\;\left(\sigma_{i,A}^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{k,A}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\sigma_{j,B}^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{l,B}\right).

On another part, we have

\displaystyle\left(\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\right)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{k\in K}\sigma_{k,A}\right)\;\textit{\rm and}\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma_{j,B}\right)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{l\in L}\sigma_{l,B}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\exists i\in I,j\in J,k\in K,l\in L,\;\left(\sigma_{i,A}^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\sigma_{k,A}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\sigma_{j,B}^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sigma_{l,B}\right)

As a conclusion, the properties
U_{A}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
U_{B}\in{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
imply
\{\,\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}\;|\;\sigma_{A}\in U_{A},\sigma_{B}\in U_{B}\,\}\in{\mathcal{K}}({\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})
.
∎

Lemma 6.13
.

We have the two required bimorphic properties for the tensor product. Explicitly, for any
\{\,\sigma_{i,A}\;|\;i\in I\,\}\subseteq{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
,
\{\,\sigma_{j,B}\;|\;j\in J\,\}\subseteq{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
,
\sigma_{A}\in{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}
and
\sigma_{B}\in{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}
, we have

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,A})\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{B}),

(6.38)

\displaystyle\sigma_{A}\widehat{\otimes}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,B})=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}).

(6.39)

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of the bimorphic properties of the minimal tensor product and the properties of the semi-lattice structure of
{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}

\displaystyle(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,A})\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{B}=\{\,\alpha_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}\;|\;\alpha_{A}\in\Theta^{{\overline{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}}(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\sigma_{i,A}),\alpha_{B}\in\Theta^{{{\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}(\sigma_{B})\,\}

(6.40)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\,(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}_{i\in I}\,\alpha_{i,A})\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}\;|\;\forall i\in I,\alpha_{i,A}\in\Theta^{{{\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}}(\sigma_{i,A}),\alpha_{B}\in\Theta^{{\overline{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}(\sigma_{B})\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}_{i\in I}\,\alpha_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}\;|\;\forall i\in I,\alpha_{i,A}\in\Theta^{{\overline{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}}(\sigma_{i,A}),\alpha_{B}\in\Theta^{{{\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}(\sigma_{B})\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\,\{\,\alpha_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha_{B}\;|\;\alpha_{i,A}\in\Theta^{{\overline{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}}(\sigma_{i,A}),\alpha_{B}\in\Theta^{{{\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}(\sigma_{B})\,\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}(\sigma_{i,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{B})

∎

Lemma 6.14
.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}\right)\in\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}},\;\forall\;\left(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\right)\in\overline{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}},

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\left(\forall{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{A},{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}\in{\mathfrak{E}}_{B},\;\;\bigwedge{}_{i\in I}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma_{i,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma_{i,B})=\bigwedge{}_{j\in J}{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{A}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A})\bullet{\epsilon}\,{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}_{{\mathfrak{l}}_{B}}(\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt\;\Rightarrow\;(\,\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}=\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widehat{\otimes}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}}_{j\in J}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,A}\widehat{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{j,B}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(6.41)

Proof.

Direct consequence of the definition of the tensor product.
∎

Lemma 6.15
.

We have the two following partial traces

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\begin{array}[]{rcrclccrcrcl}&&\zeta^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{(1)}:\;\;\;\;\;\;\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}&\longrightarrow&\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}&&&&&\zeta^{{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}_{(2)}:\;\;\;\;\;\;\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}&\longrightarrow&\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}\\
&&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}&\mapsto&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}&&&&&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{i,B}&\mapsto&\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}}_{i\in I}\sigma_{i,B}\end{array}

Proof.

Direct consequence of Theorem
4.11
.
∎

Theorem 6.16
.

The tensor product
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
defined in Definition
6.9
satisfies the requirements addressed in subsection
4.1
for the description of generic bipartite experiments.

Proof.

According to subsection
4.1
, this is a direct consequence of Lemma
6.10
, Lemma
6.11
, Lemma
6.12
, Lemma
6.13
, Lemma
6.14
, Lemma
6.15
.
∎

We note that the minimal tensor product had already proven to be adequate for the description of bipartite deterministic experiments, especially because of the property saying that the minimal tensor product of simplex spaces of states is itself a simplex space of states (Theorem
4.22
).

Now, the tensor product
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
defined in Definition
6.9
reveals to be adequate for the description of bipartite completely indeterministic experiments on a compound system build from two completely indeterministic individual systems described respectively by the ontic completions
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A},\star)\!)_{c}
and
(\!(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B},\star)\!)_{c}
. This fact is exhibited in the following theorem.

Theorem 6.17
.

If
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\star)
and
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
are completely indeterministic spaces of states, then
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
is also a completely indeterministic space of states.

Proof.

Let us consider
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\star)
and
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
two completely indeterministic spaces of states. Let us consider
\sigma:=\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B},\lambda:=\lambda_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B}\in(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}})^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\sigma\underline{\perp}\lambda
. We recall that
\sigma\underline{\perp}\lambda
means
\lambda_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(\sigma_{A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}^{\star})
which means
\lambda_{A}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}^{\star}
or
\lambda_{B}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}^{\star}
, i.e.
\sigma_{A}\underline{\perp}\lambda_{A}
or
\sigma_{B}\underline{\perp}\lambda_{B}
. Let us distinguish the three different cases.

(1)
\sigma_{A}\underline{\perp}\lambda_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}\underline{\perp}\lambda_{B}
.
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\star)
and
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}},\star)
being two completely indeterministic spaces of states, we deduce that there exists
\kappa_{A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\kappa_{B}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\kappa_{A}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\lambda_{A}
,
\kappa_{A}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\sigma_{A}
, and
\kappa_{B}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\lambda_{B}
,
\kappa_{B}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\sigma_{B}
, i.e.
\kappa_{A}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}^{\star}
and
\kappa_{A}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\lambda_{A}^{\star}
and
\kappa_{B}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\lambda_{B}^{\star}
and
\kappa_{B}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}^{\star}
. Then, we have
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(\sigma_{A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}^{\star})
and
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(\lambda_{A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B}^{\star})
, i.e.
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})
and
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}(\lambda_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B})
.

(2)
\sigma_{A}\underline{\perp}\lambda_{A}
and
\sigma_{B}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\lambda_{B}
.
(\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}},\star)
being a completely indeterministic spaces of states, we deduce that there exists
\kappa_{A}\in\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\kappa_{A}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\lambda_{A}
,
\kappa_{A}\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\sigma_{A}
, i.e.
\kappa_{A}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\sigma_{A}^{\star}
and
\kappa_{A}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\lambda_{A}^{\star}
. Let us fix
\kappa_{B}:=\lambda_{B}
. We then note that
\kappa_{B}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\lambda_{B}^{\star}
and
\kappa_{B}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\sigma_{B}^{\star}
. We then have
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(\sigma_{A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B}^{\star})
and
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}(\lambda_{A}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}\bot_{{}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B}^{\star})
, i.e.
