(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}(\sigma_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{B})
and
(\kappa_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\kappa_{B})\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}(\lambda_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}\lambda_{B})
.

(3) idem.

This concludes the proof.
∎

6.3.
Morphisms for the bipartite indeterministic experiments

Let us consider a morphism
f
(resp.
g
) from a states space
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}}}
) to another states space
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}}}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}}}
).

We can define the morphism
(f\widetilde{\otimes}g)
from the states space
{\hat{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}}
with
{\overline{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}={{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}}}
to the states space
{\hat{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}}
with
{\overline{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}={{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}}}
by

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\Theta^{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}}}((f\widetilde{\otimes}g)(\xi))=

(6.45)

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{2}}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{2}}}}(\{\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{A_{2}B_{2}}}}_{i\in I}f(\omega_{i,A_{1}})\widetilde{\otimes}g(\omega_{i,B_{1}})\;|\;(\bigsqcap{}^{{}^{{\overline{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}}}_{i\in I}\omega_{i,A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\omega_{i,B_{1}})\in\Theta^{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}}\widetilde{\otimes}{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B_{1}}}}(\xi)\;\})\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

for any
\xi\in{\hat{S}}_{A_{1}B_{1}}
.

7.
The multidimensional linear indeterministic space of states (the multi-quantum-bit system)

The previous section established a general proposal for the tensor product of indeterministic spaces of states. In the next section, we will see that this construction is sufficient to achieve our main objective (i.e. to exhibit the main typically quantum properties in our quantum logic framework). The present section is devoted to a detailed analysis of the space of states obtained as the iterated tensor product of the elementary indeterministic space of states associated with the quantum bit. Not only can this space of states be simply described using the ontic completion tools developed above, but we will also demonstrate how close this space is to an irreducible Hilbert geometry.

7.1.
Preliminary remarks

To begin this subsection, we consider the two copies of one-dimensional indeterministic spaces of states
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{\prime}_{N_{A}}
and
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{B}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{\prime}_{N_{B}}
and we consider the space of states
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}:={{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A}\widetilde{\otimes}{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{B}
. We denote
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and
{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}}
. We intent to describe the ontic completion
{{{\hat{S}}}}_{AB}:={\mathfrak{S}}_{A}\widehat{\otimes}{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}
defined according to the subsection
6.2
.
If necessary, in order to shorten our formulas, we will eventually replace the notation
{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{AB}
by
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
and the notation
{{{\hat{S}}}}{}_{AB}
by
{\mathfrak{S}}
.

Theorem 7.1
.

Let us consider
\mu,\nu,\phi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
such that
\gamma=(\phi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\phi,\nu
and
\mu^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma
, we have

\displaystyle\chi:=(\mu^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma)\;\;\textit{\rm exists in}\;{\mathfrak{S}}.

(7.1)

We have then either
\chi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
or
\chi\in{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
and then, in the last case, denoting as usual
\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi):=Max((\downarrow_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi)\cap\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
, we have

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\;\;(\exists\kappa\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;|\;\alpha\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\kappa)\;\textit{\rm and}\;\alpha\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.2)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha,\beta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\;\alpha\not=\beta,\;\;\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha,\beta,

(7.3)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha,\beta,\delta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\;\textit{\rm distinct},\;\;\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\beta)\not=(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\delta),\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.4)

Let us once again consider
\mu\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
such that
\mu^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma
.
Let us assume that there exists
\nu\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\gamma=(\mu\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\mu,\nu
then let us here again denote
\chi=(\mu^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma)
.

If we have
\chi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
, then necessarily
\chi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
, and if we have
\chi\in{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
then

\displaystyle\exists(\varphi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon},(\psi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}\in(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}})^{\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)}\;|\;

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\varphi_{\gamma}:=\mu,\psi_{\gamma}:=\nu,\;\;\forall\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\;\;\;\varphi_{\gamma}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\alpha},\;\;\varphi_{\gamma}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\alpha},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\epsilon\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\;\;\;\psi_{\epsilon},\varphi_{\epsilon}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\epsilon,\;\;\psi_{\epsilon}\not=\varphi_{\epsilon}

(7.6)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\epsilon\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\;\;\;\varphi_{\epsilon}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\epsilon},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.7)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\alpha,\beta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\alpha\not=\beta,\;\;\;\varphi_{\alpha}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\beta},\;\;\varphi_{\alpha}^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\beta},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.8)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\alpha,\beta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\alpha\not=\beta,\;\exists\lambda_{\alpha\beta}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;|\;(\varphi_{\beta}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\alpha}),(\psi_{\beta}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\alpha})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda_{\alpha\beta},\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\textit{\rm and}\;\lambda_{\alpha\beta}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\alpha},\;\;\lambda_{\alpha\beta}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\beta},

(7.9)

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt\forall\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\exists\mu_{\alpha\gamma}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;|\;(\varphi_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\varphi_{\alpha}),(\psi_{\gamma}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\alpha})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\mu_{\alpha\gamma}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle\hskip 184.9429pt\textit{\rm and}\;\mu_{\alpha\gamma}^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\psi_{\alpha},

(7.10)

The collections
(\varphi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon},(\psi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}\in(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}})^{\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)}
satisfying the properties (
7.5
)(
7.7
)(
7.8
) are unique up to a permutation on the elements of
\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\}
.

Let us once again consider
\mu\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\gamma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
such that
\mu^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma
.
If there exists
\nu\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\gamma=(\mu\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\mu,\nu
then let us here again denote
\chi=(\mu^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma)
. If we have
\chi\in{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
then

\displaystyle\forall\beta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\smallsetminus\{\gamma\},\exists\omega,\kappa\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;|\;

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt(\omega\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\kappa)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega,\kappa,\;\textit{\rm and}\;\omega^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\omega\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\kappa)=\beta\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma^{\star}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.11)

Proof.

We will consider
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime},\alpha^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\beta,\beta^{\prime},\beta^{\prime\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{B}^{{}^{pure}}
and we intent to compute

cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)

with
\alpha^{\prime}\not=\alpha^{\prime\prime}
or
\beta^{\prime}\not=\beta^{\prime\prime}
, and with
(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
.

We will distinguish different cases.

(1)
\alpha^{\prime}\not=\alpha^{\prime\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}=\beta^{\prime}
. We have
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})=\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}
and then
\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}
is the supremum of
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
. Hence,

cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=\{\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}\}\;\in\;{\mathcal{K}}({{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}})

(2)
\alpha^{\prime}=\alpha^{\prime\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}\not=\beta^{\prime}
. We have
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})=\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}
and then
\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}
is the supremum of
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
. Hence,

cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=\{\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}\}\;\in\;{\mathcal{K}}({{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}})

(3)
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\not=\alpha^{\prime}
or
\beta^{\prime\prime}\not=\beta^{\prime}
. We have to distinguish different sub-cases :

(3A)
\alpha^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
or
\beta^{\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
. Then,
\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}
is the supremum in
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
of
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
. And then,

cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=\{\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}\}\;\in\;{\mathcal{K}}({{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}})

(3B)
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
or
\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
. Then,
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}
is the supremum in
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
of
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
. And then,

cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=\{\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}\}\;\in\;{\mathcal{K}}({{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}})

(3C)
\alpha^{\prime}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
and
\beta^{\prime}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
or
\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
. Then, we note that
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
have no common upper-bound in
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
. We have then to note that
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
admit a supremum in
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
which is
(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
. We note also that
(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})
and
(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{B}}})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})
admit a supremum in
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}
which is
(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})
. Hence, we check directly that

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{c}}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=:U

\displaystyle U=\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.12)

It is then rather easy to check that
{\mathfrak{c}}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}(U)=U
. Hence,
cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}(U)=U
.
Endly, we check directly that
U\in{\mathcal{K}}({{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}})
(note that this check uses explicitly the properties
\alpha^{\prime}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
and
\beta^{\prime}\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
and
\alpha^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\alpha^{\star}
or
\beta^{\prime\prime}\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A}}}\beta^{\star}
assumed in case 3C). As a result

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{AB}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)=:U

\displaystyle U=\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.13)

As a conclusion of this case analysis we have proved the property (
7.1
). By the way we can check directly on the expressions of
cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\right\}\right)
established along this case analysis the properties (
7.2
)(
7.3
)(
7.4
) and also the properties (
7.7
)(
7.8
) if we restrict ourselves to the case
\alpha=\alpha^{\prime\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime\prime}=\beta
.

Let us summarize. If
\chi:=(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})
is in
{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Theta^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\chi\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\gamma,\delta,\epsilon\}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\gamma=(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\gamma}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta,\;\;\;\;\psi_{\gamma}=\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime},

\displaystyle\delta=(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\delta}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star},\;\;\;\;\psi_{\delta}=\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime},

\displaystyle\epsilon=(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta),

\displaystyle\varphi_{\epsilon}=\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta,\;\;\;\;\psi_{\epsilon}=\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star},

\displaystyle\lambda_{\delta\epsilon}=\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star},

\displaystyle\mu_{\gamma\delta}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime},\;\;\;\;\mu_{\gamma\epsilon}=\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta.

The collections
(\varphi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon},(\psi_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon}\in(\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}})^{\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)}
given above satisfy the properties (
7.5
)(
7.7
)(
7.8
). They are unique up to a permutation on the set
\{\delta,\epsilon\}
because of the following basic property

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\{\gamma,\delta,\epsilon\},

\displaystyle\underline{\,\alpha\,}_{{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}=\{\varphi_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha}\}

(7.15)

inherited from (
4.24
).

The properties (
7.9
)(
7.10
) can be checked directly.

Concerning the property (
7.11
), it suffice to compute

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{\left((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\right)^{\star}\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\right\}\right)=

\displaystyle=\left\{(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}{}^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}{}^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.16)

and to observe then that

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{\left((\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\right)^{\star}\,,\,(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime})\right\}\right)=

\displaystyle cl_{c}^{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}\left(\left\{(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}{}^{\star})^{\star}\,,\,(\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}{}^{\star})\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!(\alpha^{\prime}{}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star})\right\}\right)

(7.17)

∎

Now, we consider
N
copies of one-dimensional indeterministic spaces of states
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{i}}={\mathfrak{Z}}^{\prime}_{M_{A_{i}}}
for
i=1,\cdots,N
(
M_{A_{i}}\in\mathbb{N}
) and we now consider the real structure defined by
{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}\cdots A_{N}}:={{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{N}}
. We denote
{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{i}}={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{i}}}}
for
i=1,\cdots,N
. And we introduce, as before, the tensor product
{{{\hat{S}}}}_{A_{1}\cdots A_{N}}:={\mathfrak{J}}^{c}_{\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{A_{1}}\widehat{\otimes}\cdots\widehat{\otimes}\overline{{\mathfrak{S}}}_{A_{N}}}
. This space of states will be said to be the
N-
dimensional indeterministic space of states. If necessary, in order to shorten our formulas, we will denote
{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}\cdots A_{N}}
simply by
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
and
{{{\hat{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}\cdots A_{N}}
simply by
{\mathfrak{S}}
.

For simplicity, we will restrict ourselves to the case
N=3
in order to clarify a central aspect of the iteration of the tensor product.

We will then consider
\sigma_{A_{1}},\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime},\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{1}}
,
\sigma_{A_{2}},\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime},\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{2}}
,
\sigma_{A_{3}},\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime},\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{3}}
. We will suppose that
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{1}}
, and
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}},\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{3}}
.

From the following properties

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{1}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.18)

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\bot_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}_{A_{1}}}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.19)

we deduce

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\left\{\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\,,\,\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}}\right\}\sqsubseteq

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{c}}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}(\left\{(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\right\})\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.20)

Now we note that

\alpha:=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime},
and
\beta:=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}}
are two distinct elements of
{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}^{{}^{pure}}
and
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\beta)\not=\bot_{{}_{{{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}}
and that
\forall\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}^{{}^{pure}}
we have
(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}}}\gamma^{\prime})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}}}\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}
. In other words, the conditions of Theorem
3.42
are satisfied. Then,

cl^{{{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\{\alpha,\beta\})\notin{\mathcal{K}}({{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}})
.

As a result, we have obtained

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptcl_{c}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}(\left\{(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\right\})

\displaystyle\notin

\displaystyle{\mathcal{K}}({{{{\overline{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}})\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.21)

In other words, as long as
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{1}}
, and
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}},\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
, and
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}
and
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{3}}
, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\nexists\alpha\in{{{{\widetilde{S}}}}{}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle(\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\!\!\alpha\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}\;\underline{\perp}\;\alpha\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.22)

If, on the contrary, the condition (
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime}\not=\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}
) is not satisfied anymore (we maintain nevertheless the conditions
\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{1}}
,
\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}},\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}
,
\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star}\not=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}},\sigma^{\prime\prime}_{A_{3}}
because, if this condition is not satisfied, the result is easy to compute), we have then first of all

\displaystyle\hskip-71.13188pt(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\hat{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})=

\displaystyle=\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\left((\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\hat{S}}}}_{A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\right)

(7.23)

i.e.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptcl_{c}^{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}(\left\{(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\right\})=\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle\left\{(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star})\,,\,\right.

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\left.(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\right\}

(7.24)

and we have also

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt(\sigma_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}})^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{{{\hat{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{S}}}}_{A_{1}A_{2}A_{3}}}}\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})=\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star}.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.25)

The results obtained by flipping the tensors and exchanging the corresponding indices are obvious.

Let us summarize the previous result, but before that, let us introduce some notations.

Definition 7.2
.

Let us consider
\nu
and
\phi
two elements of
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
. We will define the following reflexive and symmetric binary relation

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\nu\wr\phi\;\;

\displaystyle:\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle(\,\exists I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,N\},Card(I)\geq N-2\;|\;\forall i\in I,\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\nu)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\phi)\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.26)

Definition 7.3
.

Let us fix
\nu\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
j,k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
with
j<k
.
Let us denote, for any
\kappa\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
,

\displaystyle\Upsilon^{(j,k)}_{\kappa}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(j)}(\kappa)\widetilde{\otimes}\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(k)}(\kappa)\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{j}}^{{}^{pure}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{k}}^{{}^{pure}}

(7.27)

and, for any
(\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta)\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{j}}^{{}^{pure}}\widetilde{\otimes}\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{k}}^{{}^{pure}}
,

\displaystyle\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta}:=\delta_{1}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\delta_{j-1}\widetilde{\otimes}\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\delta_{j+1}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\delta_{k-1}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta\widetilde{\otimes}\delta_{k+1}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\delta_{N}

(7.28)

with
\delta_{m}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(m)}(\nu)
for any
m\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
.

Theorem 7.4
.

Let us consider
\nu

and
\phi

two distinct elements of
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\mu

another element of
{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
. Let us assume that
\mu\not\!\!\!\underline{\perp}\nu,\phi
.
We have

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt(\,\exists\chi\in({{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})\;|\;(\,(\nu\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\phi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi\;\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;\;\chi\,\underline{\perp}\,\mu\,)\,)\;\;\Leftrightarrow

(7.29)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt(\,\exists I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,N\},Card(I)=N-2\;|\;

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt\forall i\in I,\forall\sigma,\kappa\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},(\,(\sigma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\sigma,\chi,\;\;(\kappa\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\kappa,\chi\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\sigma)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\kappa)\,).

In particular,
\exists\chi\in{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}\;|\;(\,(\nu\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\phi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi\;\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;\;\chi\,\underline{\perp}\,\mu\,)\,)
implies
\forall i\in I,\;\;\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\nu)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\phi)
.

Let us denote
j,k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
with
j<k
such that for any
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\smallsetminus\{j,k\}
we have

\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\nu)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\phi)
. Endly, let us denote
\alpha:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(j)}(\mu)
,
\beta:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(k)}(\mu)
,

\alpha^{\prime}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(j)}(\nu)
,
\beta^{\prime}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(k)}(\nu)
,

\alpha^{\prime\prime}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(j)}(\phi)
,
\beta^{\prime\prime}:=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(k)}(\phi)
.

As long as
\lambda:=\mu\sqcup_{{}_{\mathfrak{S}}}(\nu\sqcup_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\phi)
exists in
{{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}
, we have (see (
7.13
))

\displaystyle\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\{\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\!\!\!\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}}\,,\,\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime}}\,,\,\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\Delta^{(j,k);\nu}_{\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\prime\prime}}\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.30)

Proof.

Once again we will fix
N=3
.
It suffices to remark, using the expression (
7.24
), the expression of the infimum (
3.29
), the property (
7.2
) and the expansion formula (
4.24
), that the relations
(\sigma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\sigma,\chi
and
(\kappa\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\chi)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\kappa,\chi
impose necessarily
\sigma
and
\kappa
to be both elements of the following subset of pure states

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\{(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime})\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\prime\prime})\,,\,

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{2}}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star})\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\prime\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{3}}^{\star})\,,\,(\sigma_{A_{1}}^{\prime}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma_{A_{2}}^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\prime}_{A_{3}})\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.31)

This concludes the proof, which can be straightforwardly generalized to generic
N
.
∎

We end this subsection by giving some basic properties that will be fruitful in the sequel.

Theorem 7.5
.

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\forall\alpha,\beta\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}},

\displaystyle\beta\not=\alpha\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\alpha,\beta.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.32)

Proof.

The property (
7.32
) is obtained by a trivial recursion proof (on the number
N
) using Theorem
6.7
.
∎

Theorem 7.6
.

We have the following basic property

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\forall\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;\textit{\rm distinct},

\displaystyle(\,(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta)\not=(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta)\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda\,)\;\;\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.33)

Proof.

The property (
7.33
) is obtained by a trivial recursion proof (on the number
N
) using Theorem
6.8
.
∎

Theorem 7.7
.

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\forall\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\in{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;\textit{\rm distinct},(\,(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta)\not=(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta)\;\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\;(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta),(\gamma\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\delta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda\,)\;\;\Rightarrow

(7.34)

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\exists I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,N\},Card(I)=N-2\;|\;\forall i\in I,\forall\sigma,\kappa\in\{\lambda,\alpha,\beta,\gamma,\delta\},\;\;\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\sigma)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\kappa).\;\;\;\;\;\;

Proof.

Direct consequence of the expansion formula (
4.24
). Let us consider
\lambda:=\sigma^{\lambda}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\lambda}_{A_{N}}
,
\alpha:=\sigma^{\alpha}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\alpha}_{A_{N}}
,
\beta:=\sigma^{\beta}_{A_{1}}\widetilde{\otimes}\cdots\widetilde{\otimes}\sigma^{\beta}_{A_{N}}
. The property
(\alpha\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}}}}\beta)\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda
imposes

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\exists i\in\{1,\cdots,N\},\;|\;(\,\forall j\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\smallsetminus\{i\},\;\;\sigma^{\alpha}_{A_{j}}=\sigma^{\beta}_{A_{j}}=\sigma^{\lambda}_{A_{j}}\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;(\sigma^{\alpha}_{A_{j}}\sqcap_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{j}}}}}}}\!\!\sigma^{\beta}_{A_{j}})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{{{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{j}}}}}}}\sigma^{\lambda}_{A_{j}}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.35)

This leads directly to the announced result.
∎

7.2.
Towards Hilbert geometry

Let us now focus on projectivity and orthogonality properties relative to the multidimensional linear indeterministic space of states.

Definition 7.8
.

We will define the following subset of
{\mathfrak{S}}
:

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\cup\left\{\chi\in{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;|\;\exists\mu,\nu,\phi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},\;\;\;\;\gamma:=\nu\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\phi\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu,\phi,\;\;\;\;\gamma\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\mu^{\star},\;\;\;\;\chi=\mu^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\gamma\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.36)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\cup\left\{\chi\in{\mathfrak{S}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}\;|\;\exists\mu,\phi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},\;\;\mu\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\phi\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\mu,\phi,\;\;\;\;\;\chi=\mu^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\mu\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\phi)\right\}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.37)

and

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\cup({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}),

(7.38)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{G}}^{\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\cup({\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}).

(7.39)

We note obviously that
{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\supseteq{\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}
and
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\supseteq{\mathfrak{G}}^{\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Definition 7.9
.

On
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
, we will define the
consistency relation
, denoted
\asymp
, as follows (see Definition
7.2
for a definition of
\wr
):

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}},\;\;\;\;\chi\asymp\chi^{\prime}\;\;:\Leftrightarrow\;\;(\,\chi=\chi^{\prime}\;\;\textit{\rm or}\;\;\exists\epsilon\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\cap\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi^{\prime}),\;\;\epsilon=(\chi\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi^{\prime})\,)\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.40)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\chi\in{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}},\forall\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},\;\;\;\;\chi\asymp\sigma\;\;\Leftrightarrow\;\;\sigma\asymp\chi\;\;:\Leftrightarrow\;\;(\,\exists\epsilon\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\;|\;\epsilon\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma\,)

(7.41)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},\;\;\;\;\sigma\asymp\sigma^{\prime}\;\;:\Leftrightarrow\;\;\sigma\wr\sigma^{\prime}

(7.42)

Note that, due to the property (
7.32
) we have
\forall\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},(\sigma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma^{\prime})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\;\textit{\rm or }\;\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
.

Remark 7.10
.

Note that, using (
7.2
), we obtain easily

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\forall\chi,\chi^{\prime}\in{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}},

\displaystyle(\,\chi\asymp\chi^{\prime}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\chi\not=\chi^{\prime}\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;Card(\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\cap\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi^{\prime}))=1.

(7.43)

Remark 7.11
.

Note that, using (
7.2
), we obtain easily

\displaystyle\hskip-34.14322pt\forall\chi\in{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}},\forall\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}},

\displaystyle(\,\chi\asymp\sigma\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;Card(\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\cap(\downarrow_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma))=1.

(7.44)

Definition 7.12
.

A
consistent subset of states
is defined to be a subset of
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that all its elements are pairwise consistent.

Definition 7.13
.

The
consistency cover
of
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
, denoted
{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
, is defined as a family of maximal consistent subsets covering the whole set of elements of
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}(\bigcup_{{}_{U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}}}U)={\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\\
\forall U,U’\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\;\;\;U\subseteq U’\;\Rightarrow\;U=U’\\
\forall U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\;\forall\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in U,\;\sigma\asymp\sigma^{\prime}.\end{array}\right.

Definition 7.14
.

Let us consider any consistent subset
U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

We define a ternary relation
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}
on
U
(it will be called
colinearity
) as follows

\displaystyle\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in U,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})\;\;:\Leftrightarrow\;\;(\,\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{3}\;\textit{\rm or}\;(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{1}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.49)

Lemma 7.15
.

We note the following sheaf condition

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\forall U,U^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},

\displaystyle\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in U\cap U^{\prime}\;\Rightarrow\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})={\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.50)

Let us begin by checking a basic symmetry result of the colinearity relation.

Lemma 7.16
.

The ternary relation
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}
defined on the consistent subset
U
is symmetric under any permutation of its arguments.

Proof.

It suffices to prove that it is symmetric under the exchange of the two first arguments (because it is already symmetric under the exchange of its two last arguments). This is a simple computation. We reproduce only one of its cases.

Let us then consider
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}
are pairwise compatible. We will choose for example
\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}}
.

Let us first consider that
\sigma_{2}\not=\sigma_{3}
. Then,
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
is equivalent to
\sigma_{1}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
, which means that there exists
\epsilon\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{1}
where
\{\epsilon\}=\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{2})\cap\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{3})
(here we use property (
7.43
) or (
7.44
)). We then deduce that
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})=\epsilon
and using
\epsilon\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{2})
we obtain
\sigma_{2}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
from (
7.2
). This proves
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
.

Let us now consider that
\sigma_{2}=\sigma_{3}
. Then, we have immediately
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
. We have also trivially
\sigma_{2}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})
which means that we have also
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{2},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
.
∎

Lemma 7.17
.

[Veblen-Young’s first geometric property]

\displaystyle\forall U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in U,

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{2}).

(7.51)

Proof.

Trivial.
∎

Before studying the different properties of the colinearity relation, we begin to prove an important lemma (Lemma
7.19
) describing the structure of the consistent subsets. But before that, we will check a small technical result.

Lemma 7.18
.

Let us consider
\chi\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
. We have

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\sigma_{1}\not=\sigma_{2},\;\;\;\;\sigma_{1}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}=\chi.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.52)

Proof.

From property (
7.2
) we know that

\forall\sigma\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\;\;\sigma\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi
. Then, if we consider
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)
with
\sigma_{1}\not=\sigma_{2}
, we have
\sigma_{1}\sqsubset_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi
and
\sigma_{1}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi
. Then, we conclude that
(\sigma_{1}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})=\chi
.
∎

Lemma 7.19
.

Let
U\subseteq{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
be a consistent subset of states, such that there exists
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4},\lambda\in U
satisfying

\displaystyle\forall i,j\in\{1,2,3,4\},i\not=j,

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}\not=\sigma_{j}

(7.53)

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,2,3,4\},

\displaystyle\sigma_{i}\not=\lambda

(7.54)

\displaystyle\forall i,j,k,l\in\{1,2,3,4\},\{i,j\}\not=\{k,l\},

\displaystyle(\sigma_{i}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{j})\not=(\sigma_{k}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{l}),

(7.55)

\displaystyle\lambda\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}),

\displaystyle\lambda\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4}),

(7.56)

then we have necessarily

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,2,3,4\},\;\sigma_{i}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}.

(7.57)

Proof.

The proof is in the Appendix.
∎

Lemma 7.20
.

Let us consider
U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that there exists
\chi\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
with
\chi\in U
. The subset
U
is necessarily of one of the two following types

•

(Consistent Type 1)
\exists\textit{\bf!}\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)
such that
\forall\sigma\in U
we have
(\sigma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi)=\alpha
.

•

(Consistent Type 2)
\exists\alpha,\beta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\alpha\not=\beta
and
\exists\sigma,\sigma^{\prime}\in U\cap\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
(\sigma\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi)=\alpha
and
(\sigma^{\prime}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\chi)=\beta
.

Proof.

Direct consequence of Lemma
7.19
and the assumption of maximality on
U
.
∎

Lemma 7.21
.

Let us consider a maximal consistent subset of states
U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
and let us assume that there exists
\lambda\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that
\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U
and such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
and
\forall\mu,\nu,\rho\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\neg\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\mu,\nu,\rho)
. Then, the consistent subset
U
is necessarily of consistent type 2.

Proof.

As long as
\lambda\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
,
\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\lambda)
is given as in Theorem
7.4
.

The conditions
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
and
\forall\mu,\nu,\rho\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\neg\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\mu,\nu,\rho)
impose the existence of
\epsilon,\delta\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\lambda)
such that
\epsilon\not=\delta
and
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})=\epsilon
and
(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})=\delta
. We note that these conditions impose also the distinctness of the elements
\sigma_{i}
, i.e.
\alpha^{\prime}\not=\alpha^{\prime\prime}
and
\beta^{\prime}\not=\beta^{\prime\prime}
.
∎

Let us now come back to the properties of the colinearity relation.

Lemma 7.22
.

[Veblen-Young’s second geometric property]

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U,

\displaystyle(\,{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.58)

Proof.

This is a straightforward check. We reproduce one of its cases. Let us then consider for example
\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
with
\sigma_{3}\not=\sigma_{4}
.

Then,
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
is equivalent to
\sigma_{1}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
or, using property (
7.43
),
\epsilon\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{1}
with
\{\epsilon\}=\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{3})\cap\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{4})
. Analogously,
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
is equivalent to
\sigma_{2}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
or, using property (
7.43
),
\mu\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}
with
\{\mu\}=\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{3})\cap\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\sigma_{4})
. Using property (
7.43
), we also know that
\mu=\epsilon
. We then note that
\mu=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
and then
\sigma_{1}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
, i.e.
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
.
∎

Lemma 7.23
.

[Veblen-Young’s third geometric property]

We will consider
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
. We have the following result

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\,\exists U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U,\exists\lambda\in U\;|\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})\,)\;\;\Rightarrow\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

\displaystyle\hskip-42.67912pt(\,(\exists U^{\prime},U^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\;|\;\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U^{\prime},U^{\prime\prime})\;\textit{\rm and}\;\exists\mu\in U^{\prime}\;|\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4})

\displaystyle\hskip 99.58464pt\textit{\rm and}\;\exists\chi\in U^{\prime\prime}\;|\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{4})\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.59)

Proof.

Let us first note that if
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})=(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
then we have also
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
and also
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
and then the proof of (
7.59
) is trivial. We will then assume
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})\not=(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
and
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})\not=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
and
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})\not=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
.

We will also assume
\lambda\not=\sigma_{1},\lambda\not=\sigma_{2},\lambda\not=\sigma_{3},\lambda\not=\sigma_{4}
. Indeed, if we have for example
\lambda=\sigma_{1}
then it suffices to take
\mu:=\sigma_{4}
and
\chi:=\sigma_{3}
to satisfy the announced properties.

We will then assume the existence of
\lambda\in U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
.

The assumption
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})\;\textit{\rm and}\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
is equivalent to

\displaystyle(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda,

\displaystyle(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\lambda.

(7.60)

We have then, using (
7.3
) (if
\lambda\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
) or using (
7.33
) (if
\lambda\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
), the properties

\displaystyle(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsupseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}),(\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})

(7.61)

We know from Theorem
7.7
and Theorem
7.4
that

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055pt\exists I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,N\},Card(I)=N-2\;|\;\forall i\in I,\forall\alpha,\beta\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\;\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\alpha)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\beta).

(7.62)

Having noted the property
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})\not=(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
, we will consider any
\xi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\xi\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star},

\displaystyle\xi\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star},\;\;\;\;\;\;\forall i\in I,\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\xi)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\sigma_{1})\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.63)

(we note that
\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}
and
\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4}
are different from
\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}
).

From (
7.1
), and using
\xi\not\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star}
and property (
7.62
), we know that there exists
\upsilon\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that

\displaystyle\upsilon:=\xi^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})

(7.64)

From (
7.2
), we know that
\upsilon\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
and then
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\upsilon,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4})
.
We intent to show that we have also
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\upsilon,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
.

We then consider any
\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that

\displaystyle\omega\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star},

\displaystyle\omega\not=\xi,\;\;\;\;\;\;\forall i\in I,\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\omega)=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(i)}(\xi)\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.65)

and we intent to show that
\omega^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\upsilon
(we note that

(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star}
is not in
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
and then, such an
\omega
different from
\xi
always exists).

We first note that
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}
. Indeed, if we had
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}
, and using the fact that
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})
, we would have
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})
. But now, using (
7.61
), we have in particular
\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}=(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
. As a consequence, we would have
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
, and then
\xi^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
which is false by assumption (
7.63
).

Then, from (
7.1
), and using the fact that
\sigma_{2}
is in
\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
(consequence of Lemma
7.19
) and the fact that
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)^{\star}\not\sqsubseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}
and also the property (
7.65
), we know that there exists
\nu\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that

\displaystyle\nu:=\sigma_{2}^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega).

(7.66)

Using
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star}
, we deduce as before

\displaystyle\nu\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star}=(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star}

(7.67)

(we note using, for example, the property (
7.3
) and (
7.60
) that
(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
is different from
\bot_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}
).

We then deduce

\displaystyle\nu\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star}.

(7.68)

From this inequality we deduce that

\displaystyle\exists\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\nu)

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\alpha\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star}.

(7.69)

Let us fix one of these
\alpha
.

We recall that, as long as
\xi\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
we have
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu)=Max(\{\,\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\kappa\;|\;\kappa\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\nu)\,\})
. Moreover, we have
(\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega)\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\nu)
. Then, using (
7.2
) we deduce as well

\displaystyle\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\xi\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\nu.

(7.70)

As a result, we obtain for the chosen
\alpha

\displaystyle\xi\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega

\displaystyle\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}

\displaystyle\xi\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha.

(7.71)

Now, since
\upsilon\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\xi^{\star}
and
\upsilon\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})
and
\alpha\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{2}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{4})^{\star}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\upsilon\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\xi^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha^{\star}).

(7.72)

and then using (
7.71
)

\displaystyle\upsilon\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\xi^{\star}\sqcup_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\star})\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\omega^{\star}

(7.73)

We have then obtained the announced intermediary result : for any
\omega\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}
such that the conditions (
7.65
) are satisfied, we have
\omega^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\upsilon
. We note that we have also by assumption
\xi\sqsupseteq_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})^{\star}
and
\xi^{\star}\sqsubseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\upsilon
.

Hence, we can conclude

\displaystyle\upsilon\sqsupseteq_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{3})

(7.74)

i.e. explicitly
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\upsilon,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
. Obviously, we have
\upsilon\asymp\sigma_{1},\upsilon\asymp\sigma_{2},\upsilon\asymp\sigma_{3},\upsilon\asymp\sigma_{4}
and then the existence of the consistent subset
U^{\prime}
does not pose any problem.

As a final conclusion, we obtain the existence of
\mu:=\upsilon\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4})
.

By exchanging the roles of
\sigma_{3}
and
\sigma_{4}
in our construction, we obtain also the existence of
\chi\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{4})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
.
∎

Let us now summarize what we have found so far.

Theorem 7.24
.

{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
is equipped with a structure of projective sheaf.
In other words, apart from the sheaf condition (
7.50
), we have proved the Veblen-Young’s geometric axioms of projective spaces (
7.51
)(
7.58
)(
7.59
).

Proof.

This result summarizes Lemma
7.15
, Lemma
7.17
, Lemma
7.22
, Lemma
7.23
.
∎

Definition 7.25
.

For any pair
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
, we define the line
\ell_{(\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2})}
to be the set of elements
\sigma_{3}
in
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{1}
or
\sigma_{3}=\sigma_{2}
or
\sigma_{3}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}(\sigma_{1}\sqcap_{{}_{{\mathfrak{S}}}}\sigma_{2})
.

Definition 7.26
.

For any triple
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
distinct, such that
\sigma_{i}\notin\ell_{(\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k})}
for any
\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}
, we define the plane
\wp_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})}
to be the set of elements
\sigma_{4}
in
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that there exists
i,j,k
with
\{i,j,k\}=\{1,2,3\}
and

\lambda\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
and
\lambda\in\ell_{(\sigma_{i}\sigma_{4})}
and
\lambda\in\ell_{(\sigma_{j}\sigma_{k})}
.

The set of planes on
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
will be denoted
\wp^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Any element of
\wp^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
has a structure of a projective sheaf.

Remark 7.27
.

Any plane is stable under the application of the third Veblen-Young’s property. In other words, if there exists
U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
and
\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
such that
\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U
and such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})
and
\forall\mu,\nu,\rho\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\neg\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\mu,\nu,\rho)
. Then,
\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}
are in a same plane
\wp
. Moreover, if we introduce
U^{\prime}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
and the element
\mu\in U^{\prime}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{3})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime}}(\mu,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{4})
, and if we introduce
U^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}

and
\chi\in U^{\prime\prime}
such that
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})
and
{\mathfrak{r}}^{U^{\prime\prime}}(\chi,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{4})\,)
we have necessarily
\mu,\chi\in\wp
.

Let us now consider the properties of the orthogonality relation induced on
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
from the orthogonality relation defined on
{\mathfrak{S}}
(see subsection
2.4
).

Even though we have found a very interesting projective structure on
{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
, we must now proceed with caution. Indeed, the problem with
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
is due to the following simple fact (see Remark
7.34
)

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\forall U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}},\forall\chi\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\cap U,\forall\sigma\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\cap U,

\displaystyle(\,\exists\lambda\in U\;|\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\chi,\sigma,\lambda)\;\textit{\rm and}\;\chi\underline{\perp}\lambda\,)\Rightarrow(\,\chi\in{\mathfrak{G}}^{\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}\,)\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.75)

The following sequence of results is the consequence of our attempt to deal with this annoying problem and with the necessity to restrict ourselves to
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Definition 7.28
.

A consistent subset of states
U\subseteq{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
is said to be
orthogonally complete
iff

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in U,\;\textit{\rm distinct},\;\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})\;\Rightarrow\;(\,\sigma_{1}\underline{\perp}\sigma_{2}\;\textit{\rm or}\;\sigma_{1}\underline{\perp}\sigma_{3}\;\textit{\rm or}\;\sigma_{2}\underline{\perp}\sigma_{3}\,).\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.76)

\displaystyle\hskip-62.59596pt\forall\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\in U\;|\;(\,\exists\lambda\in U,\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}),\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\lambda,\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4})\;\textit{\rm and}\;\forall\mu,\nu,\rho\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\neg\;{\mathfrak{r}}^{U}(\mu,\nu,\rho)\,)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\exists\alpha,\beta,\gamma\in\{\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3},\sigma_{4}\},\;\alpha\underline{\perp}\beta\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\alpha\underline{\perp}\gamma.\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.77)

Definition 7.29
.

For any
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}{pure}}
satisfying the following conditions

(1)

there exists
I\subseteq\{1,\cdots,N\},Card(I)=N-2\;|\;

\forall m\in I,\;\;\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(m)}(\sigma_{1})=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(m)}(\sigma_{2})=\zeta^{{}^{{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{1}}\cdots{\mathfrak{S}}_{A_{N}}}}_{(m)}(\sigma_{3})
,

(2)

if we denote
j,k\in\{1,\cdots,N\}\smallsetminus I
with
j<k
, there exists
\alpha\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{j}}^{{}^{pure}}
and
\beta\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}_{A_{k}}^{{}^{pure}}
such that

\Upsilon^{(j,k)}_{\sigma_{1}}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
,
\Upsilon^{(j,k)}_{\sigma_{2}}=\alpha^{\star}\widetilde{\otimes}\beta
,
\Upsilon^{(j,k)}_{\sigma_{3}}=\alpha\widetilde{\otimes}\beta^{\star}
.

we say that the plane
\wp_{(\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2},\sigma_{3})}
is a
starred plane
. The set of starred planes on
{\mathfrak{G}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
will be denoted
\wp_{\star}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
.

Let us now analyze the structure of orthogonally complete consistent subsets in the following lemmas.

Lemma 7.30
.

Let us consider a consistent subset of states
U\in{\mathfrak{E}}^{\check{\mathfrak{S}}}
and let us assume that there exists
\chi\in({\check{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
and that the consistent subset
U
is of consistent type 2.

Let us now assume that
U
is orthogonally complete. Then, we have necessarily
\chi\in({\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
.

Conversely, let us assume that
\chi\in({\widecheck{\mathfrak{S}}}\smallsetminus\overline{\mathfrak{S}})
and that
U
is maximal, then we have

\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636ptU=\{\chi\}\cup\{\,\varphi_{\alpha}\;|\;\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\,\}\cup\{\,\psi_{\alpha}\;|\;\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi)\,\}

(7.78)

\displaystyle\forall\alpha\in\Theta^{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}(\chi),\;\varphi_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha}\in\overline{\mathfrak{S}}{}^{{}^{pure}}\;\textit{\rm and}\;\varphi_{\alpha},\psi_{\alpha}\raisebox{5.59721pt}{\,\rotatebox{180.0}{$\sqsubseteq$}}\,_{{}_{\overline{\mathfrak{S}}}}\alpha\;\;\textit{\rm and}\;\;\varphi_{\alpha}\not=\psi_{\alpha}\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;\;

(7.79)
